SAP HANA, S/4HANA, ABAP on HANA

8 short levels: from "where does HANA sit?" to "how do I change my code?"

😫 PROBLEMReports were slow. Data sat on disk, and ABAP added numbers in loops.
💡 IDEAKeep data in memory, store it by column, let the database do the maths.
🔧 HOWHANA database + S/4HANA tables + SUM/GROUP BY in SQL and CDS views.
➡️ NEXTCDS views (Module 10) and performance tuning (Module 05).

MAPThree names, three things

🗄️
A DATABASESAP HANA
Where data is stored. Like Oracle, made by SAP.
🏢
AN ERP SOFTWARES/4HANA
New SAP business system after ECC. Runs only on HANA.
👨‍💻
A CODING STYLEABAP on HANA
Write ABAP so the fast database does the heavy work.

LEARNLevel by level

Part 1 · What HANA and S/4 are (Q54) Part 2 · Inside HANA, S/4 tables Part 3 · SQL and code pushdown (Q57)

🏗️ Every SAP system has 3 floors

📦
GR example. You post a GR in MIGO with movement type 101 (screen) → ABAP checks the PO, quantity and plant (application) → saved in MKPF/MSEG (database). HANA replaces only the bottom floor.
🧠 Remember: HANA replaces only the bottom floor. Screen → ABAP (app server) → database.

🚗 ECC vs HANA vs S/4HANA

🔧 HANA = the engine. A database only.
🚙 ECC = the old car. Could run on any engine: Oracle, DB2, MS SQL, even HANA.
🏎️ S/4HANA = the new car, built only for the HANA engine.
🧪 Suite on HANA (2013) = old ECC car with the new engine put in. Same ECC tables.

🔢 What does "S/4" mean?

R/11973, one mainframe
R/21979, mainframe + terminals
R/31992, 3 floors · ECC 6.0 = last R/3
S/42015, Simple + 4th generation

👉 S = Simple (fewer tables, no duplicate totals). 4 = 4th generation after R/1, R/2, R/3. HANA = runs only on HANA.

TopicECCS/4HANA
🗄️ DatabaseAny (Oracle, DB2, MS SQL…)HANA only
📦 GR storageMKPF + MSEG + stock totalsMATDOC, stock calculated live
💰 FinanceBSEG + index/total tablesACDOCA
👥 Vendor / customerXK01 / XD01Business Partner BP
🖥️ ScreensSAP GUIFiori apps + SAP GUI
☁️ WhereOn-premiseOn-premise, private or public cloud
📅 SupportMainstream ends 2027 (extended to 2030)Ongoing
✅ For your GR report: on S/4, SELECT … FROM mseg still works. SAP gives a compatibility view with the old name that reads from MATDOC.
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q54)

"HANA is SAP's in-memory database. ECC could run on any database, but S/4HANA, the 4th generation ERP after R/3, runs only on HANA. Because HANA totals data fast, S/4 removed duplicate tables: material documents moved from MKPF and MSEG into MATDOC, and finance items into ACDOCA. Old table names still work through compatibility views."

🧠 Remember: HANA = engine, ECC = old car (any engine), S/4HANA = new car (HANA only). S = Simple, 4 = 4th generation.

🐢 Before HANA: ECC on Oracle / DB2

  • 💽 Data on disk. Disk is slow to read.
  • 📄 Stored row by row: a whole invoice line sits together.
  • 🚚 Data to code: pull rows up to the app server, then loop and add in ABAP.
📦
Report: "total GR quantity per material this year". SELECT * pulls every MSEG line, all 200+ columns, then LOOP + COLLECT. 5 lakh lines travel to make 300 totals.

😫 Problems

  • 🐌 Big reports ran as night jobs.
  • ➕ Extra total tables (GLT0, KNC1) and index tables (BSIS, BSAS) had to be updated on every posting.
  • 🧊 Pooled / cluster tables saved space but were hard to query.
🧠 Remember: Before: disk + rows + "data to code" = slow night jobs and extra total tables.

🚀 Three tricks inside HANA

🧠 In-memory: working data in RAM. Disk only keeps a safe copy.
📊 Column store: each column stored together, compresses well.
⚡ Parallel: one query runs on many CPU cores at once.

🎮 Try it: SUM(qty) of GR lines

Cells read: 0
💡 Row store opens every cell. Column store reads only Qty. On 1 crore rows the gap is huge.
🧠 Remember: HANA is fast because of RAM + Columns + Parallel = "RCP".

🏢 S/4HANA = ECC rebuilt for HANA

HANA can total crores of rows live, so SAP removed the extra total and index tables and rewrote the ERP. S = Simple, 4 = 4th generation.

AreaECCS/4HANA
💰 FinanceBSEG + BSIS/BSAS + GLT0ACDOCA
📦 GR / stockMKPF + MSEG + totalsMATDOC
👥 PartnersKNA1, LFA1Business Partner (BP)
🧊 Table kindsPooled, clusterAll transparent
🔢 Material no.18 chars40 chars
✅ Old names like MSEG still work in SELECT via compatibility views.
🧠 Remember: S/4: MATDOC, ACDOCA, BP, all transparent, material 40 chars. Old names still work through compatibility views.

🗣️ The SQL family: who is who

SQL = the common language every database understands. ABAP has its own "versions" of it.

NameWhat it isFunny trick
🌍 SQLStandard database languageThe mother tongue
🐬 MySQLA separate database product, like Oracle. SAP ERP does not use itA different restaurant. Not our kitchen ⚠️
🏚️ Native SQLDB-specific SQL inside EXEC SQL … ENDEXEC. Skips ABAP checksLocal dialect: works in one village (one DB) only
🧑 Open SQL (classic)ABAP's own SQL. SAP translates it for any DB. Handles client + buffer for youHindi: understood everywhere via the translator
🧒 New Open SQL / ABAP SQLFrom 7.40 SP05: commas, @, inline DATA(), SUM, CASE, joinsSame Hindi, but smart: does maths at the DB
📜 CDS viewSQL-like model saved in the dictionary (ADT). Reusable, with annotations and associationsA saved recipe: write once, cook everywhere
⚡ SQLScriptHANA's own language. Used inside AMDPHANA's secret language, only for very hard dishes
Your Open SQL→DB interface translates→Native SQL of Oracle / HANA

📦 Same GR question, 4 ways

* Classic Open SQL: no commas, no @
SELECT mblnr mjahr matnr menge
  FROM mseg INTO TABLE lt_gr
  WHERE bwart = '101'.
* New Open SQL: DB adds the quantities
SELECT FROM mseg
  FIELDS matnr, SUM( menge ) AS gr_qty
  WHERE bwart = '101'
  GROUP BY matnr
  INTO TABLE @DATA(lt_qty).
// CDS view entity (ADT): saved, reusable
define view entity ZI_GR_QTY
  as select from matdoc
{ key matnr, key meins,
  @Semantics.quantity.unitOfMeasure: 'meins'
  sum( menge ) as GrQty }
where bwart = '101'
group by matnr, meins
* Native SQL: you must handle the client yourself
EXEC SQL.
  SELECT COUNT(*) INTO :lv_cnt FROM mseg
   WHERE mandt = :sy-mandt AND bwart = '101'
ENDEXEC.
⚠️ Interview trap: "Do we use MySQL in SAP?" No. MySQL is a separate database product. In ABAP we write Open SQL (ABAP SQL), and SAP turns it into the database's own SQL.
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q57)

"In ABAP we use Open SQL, now called ABAP SQL. It is database-independent: the database interface translates it, and it handles client and buffering automatically. Native SQL in EXEC SQL is tied to one database and skips those checks, so we avoid it. From 7.40, new Open SQL added commas, @ host variables, inline declarations and aggregates for code pushdown. For reusable models we use CDS views, and for very complex HANA logic, SQLScript inside AMDP."

🧠 Remember: Open SQL = Hindi with a translator, New Open SQL = smart Hindi (@, commas, SUM), CDS = saved recipe, AMDP = HANA's secret language, MySQL = not SAP.

👨‍💻 Code to data (code pushdown)

DB is now the fastest floor, so send the logic down and get back only the answer.

* pull ALL GR lines, add in ABAP
SELECT * FROM mseg INTO TABLE lt_mseg
  WHERE bwart = '101'.
LOOP AT lt_mseg INTO ls_mseg.
  ls_sum-matnr = ls_mseg-matnr.
  ls_sum-menge = ls_mseg-menge.
  COLLECT ls_sum INTO lt_sum.
ENDLOOP.
* HANA adds, returns only totals
SELECT matnr, SUM( menge ) AS gr_qty
  FROM mseg
  WHERE bwart = '101'
  GROUP BY matnr
  INTO TABLE @DATA(lt_sum).
" , between fields · @ = ABAP variable

🪜 3 tools, easiest first (tap)

🧠 Remember: Code to data: SUM / GROUP BY in SQL, not LOOP + COLLECT in ABAP.

📏 Coding rules on HANA

  • ❌ No SELECT *: column store must rebuild every column for a full row.
  • ✅ SUM / COUNT / GROUP BY in SQL, not LOOP + COLLECT.
  • ✅ JOIN instead of SELECT inside LOOP.
  • 🔍 Prove it in ST05 (SQL trace) or SAT (runtime analysis).
🎤 INTERVIEW

"SAP HANA is SAP's in-memory, column-based database, and S/4HANA is the ERP built only for it. Since the database is now the fastest layer, we push logic down: aggregations and joins in new Open SQL and CDS views, AMDP only for complex logic. In my GR report I replaced SELECT * on MSEG with LOOP and COLLECT by one SELECT with SUM and GROUP BY, and checked it in ST05."

⚠️ Don't say "HANA is the new ERP". HANA = database, S/4HANA = ERP.
🧠 Remember: No SELECT *, totals in SQL, JOIN instead of SELECT in LOOP, prove it in ST05 / SAT.

😂 HANA tricks

  • 🏗️ 3 floors = shop counter, kitchen, godown: screen, app server (ABAP), database (HANA).
  • ⚡ RCP = RAM, Columns, Parallel: the 3 reasons HANA is fast.
  • 🔢 S/4 = "Simple, 4th" after R/1, R/2, R/3. ECC 6.0 = last R/3.
  • 🏠 MKPF + MSEG → MATDOC: "do ghar ek ho gaye" (two houses became one).
  • 💰 BSEG + BSIS + BSAS + GLT0 → ACDOCA: one "Universal Journal" for all of finance.
  • 🪜 Pushdown ladder: Open SQL → CDS → AMDP = "chhota, bada, sabse bada hathiyaar" (small, big, biggest weapon).
  • 🐬 MySQL = "dusra restaurant": a different database, not used inside SAP ERP.
💼 Your project: ECC on a HANA database (Suite on HANA): HANA engine, old ECC tables (MSEG, BSEG). Not S/4.

🎮 Your turn (answer first, then tap)

🎤 FULL MODULE ANSWER

"SAP HANA is SAP's in-memory, column-based database; S/4HANA is the fourth-generation ERP that runs only on it, with simplified tables like MATDOC and ACDOCA, while old names still work through compatibility views. ECC can also run on HANA, which my project did. Because the database is now the fastest layer, I push logic down: new Open SQL with aggregates and joins first, then CDS views, and AMDP only for complex logic, avoiding SELECT * and checking the gain in ST05."

DOHands-on

  1. ADT: class ZCL_HANA_DEMO with interface IF_OO_ADT_CLASSRUN.
  2. In main: SELECT … SUM … GROUP BY on ZPO_HEADER_K.
  3. out->write( lt_sum ). then F9 to run.
  4. Expect one line per group, not every row.
  1. System → Status: check "Database system".
  2. SE38: two test programs with old and new code above.
  3. Run with ST05 trace on. Compare rows and time.
  4. On HANA systems, try SE16H for totals with no code.

TESTQuick check

1. What is SAP HANA?

HANA is the database floor. S/4HANA is the ERP.

2. Why is SUM(amount) faster in a column store?

Only one column is scanned.

3. Which pushdown tool first?

Open SQL → CDS → AMDP.

4. Where are GR items stored in S/4HANA?

MATDOC. MSEG still readable through a compatibility view.

5. In new Open SQL, what does @ mark?

@ = host variable from ABAP, e.g. @p_ebeln, @DATA(lt_gr).

6. Your project ran ECC on a HANA database. That is…

Same ECC tables (MSEG, BSEG), new database engine.
Answer all 6 first.

🗄️ 3 names, 3 things

  • HANA = database (in-memory, column). Replaces only the bottom floor.
  • S/4HANA = new ERP after ECC (2015). Runs only on HANA. S = Simple, 4 = 4th gen after R/1, R/2, R/3.
  • ABAP on HANA = code style: let the DB do the maths.
  • 🏗️ 3 floors: screen (MIGO) → app server (ABAP) → database.

🚀 Why HANA is fast

🧠 RAM, not disk · 📊 column store (reads only needed columns) · ⚡ many CPU cores in parallel.

  • New Open SQL (7.40 SP05) = commas, @, inline DATA( ), SUM, CASE.
  • CDS = saved model · AMDP = SQLScript inside HANA · Native SQL (EXEC SQL) = avoid.

In my GR report I replaced SELECT * with LOOP and COLLECT by one SELECT with SUM and GROUP BY, and checked it in ST05."

ABAP basics

The things you use every day, now with names, reasons and interview words. T-codes: SE38, SE80, SE11, SE16N.

😫 PROBLEMReports get slow when they search rows inside loops and copy every row. Basic words get mixed up in interviews.
💡 IDEAKnow your containers (structure, internal table), pick the right table type, point at rows instead of copying.
🔧 HOWProgram types, STANDARD / SORTED / HASHED tables, inline DATA( ), FIELD-SYMBOL, the right SELECT.
➡️ NEXTModule 02: Data Dictionary.

LEARNLevel by level

Part 1 · ABAP & programs (Q1, Q2) Part 2 · Data containers (Q3, Q6, Q8) Part 3 · Pointers (Q5) Part 4 · Database (Q4, Q7)
PART 1 · ABAP

🤔 What is ABAP and why is it used? (Q1)

ABAP = Advanced Business Application Programming. SAP's own programming language. All of SAP ERP (MIGO, ME21N, MIRO…) is written in it.

  • ❓ Why? SAP gives standard processes, but every company needs extra: own reports, interfaces, forms, checks. ABAP builds them.
  • ⚙️ Where it runs: on the application server (middle floor, HANA tab L1), not on your PC.
  • 🗄️ Database-independent: you write Open SQL; SAP translates it for Oracle, DB2 or HANA.

🧰 What ABAP developers build: RICEFW

LetterObjectExampleModule
RReportsGR ALV report📊 ALV
IInterfacesIDoc, files in AL11, OData📨 IDoc, 🌐 OData
CConversionsLoad vendors / materials (BDC, BAPI)📥 BDC
EEnhancementsBADI in MIGO🔌 BADI
FFormsGR slip SmartForm🧾 Forms
WWorkflowsGR approval🌐 OData
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q1)

"ABAP is SAP's programming language for business applications. It runs on the application server, accesses the database through database-independent Open SQL, and is used to build RICEFW objects: reports, interfaces, conversions, enhancements, forms and workflows. In my project I built and supported ABAP interface programs that imported goods receipt and invoice files, matched them and created postings."

🧠 Remember: ABAP = SAP's language, runs on the app server, Open SQL, builds RICEFW.

🗂️ Types of ABAP programs (Q2)

Every ABAP object is stored as a "program" (table TRDIR, field SUBC). The type says how it can start.

TypeNameStarts howT-code
1📊 Executable (report)F8, SA38, own T-code, background jobSE38
M🖥️ Module poolOnly by a dialog T-code (screens)SE80, SE51
F🧩 Function groupIts FMs are calledSE37, SE80
K🎯 Class poolIts methods are calledSE24, ADT
J📜 Interface poolImplemented by classesSE24
I📎 IncludeNever alone: pasted into othersSE38
S🔁 Subroutine poolIts FORMs are called from outsideSE38
T📦 Type group (old)Holds TYPES / CONSTANTS (e.g. SLIS)SE11
🚨 PDF fix: J = interface pool, T = type group. Not the other way round.
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q2)

"The program type decides how a program can be started: type 1 executable reports run with F8 or as jobs, M module pools run through dialog transactions, F function groups hold function modules, K and J are class and interface pools, I includes can't run alone, S are subroutine pools and T type groups hold types. My import programs were type 1 reports run as background jobs."

🧠 Remember: 1-M-F-K-J-I-S-T = "1 Mango For Kids, Juice In Steel Tumbler" 🥭.
PART 2 · CONTAINERS

📦 Structure vs internal table (Q6)

Both live in memory while your program runs.

  • 🧾 Structure = one row with many fields. Like one line of a GR: doc no., material, qty.
  • 📚 Internal table = many rows of the same structure. Like all lines of a GR file.
TYPES: BEGIN OF ty_gr,
         mblnr TYPE mblnr,   " material doc no.
         matnr TYPE matnr,   " material
         menge TYPE menge_d, " quantity
       END OF ty_gr.
DATA: ls_gr TYPE ty_gr,                     " one row  (structure)
      lt_gr TYPE STANDARD TABLE OF ty_gr.   " many rows (internal table)

🗄️ The other meaning: DDIC structure vs DDIC table

DDIC structure (SE11)DDIC transparent table (SE11)
Stores data?No. Only a shapeYes, rows in the database
UseTypes for programs, screens, interfaces (e.g. BAPI parameters)Business data (MARA, MSEG, your Y tables)
Technical settingsNoneData class, size, buffering
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q6)

"In a program, a structure holds one row and an internal table holds many rows of the same type in memory. In the dictionary, a structure only defines a shape and stores no data, while a transparent table stores rows in the database and has technical settings. In my import programs, each file line was mapped into a structure and collected in an internal table before being saved into staging tables."

🧠 Remember: structure = one row / only shape. Table = many rows / stores data.

🕰️ Work area → inline declaration (Q8)

A work area is a structure used to hold one row of an internal table while you read or change it.

EraStyleProblem
👴 OldTable with header line: table and work area share one nameConfusing (lt_gr or lt_gr[]?), banned in classes
🧑 ClassicSeparate work area wa_gr declared on topMany declarations far from use
🧒 7.40+Inline: INTO DATA(ls_gr)Declared where used ✅
LOOP AT lt_gr INTO DATA(ls_gr).          " ls_gr = work area (a copy)
  ls_gr-menge = ls_gr-menge * 2.
  MODIFY lt_gr FROM ls_gr.             " copy back, or the change is lost
ENDLOOP.
READ TABLE lt_gr INTO DATA(ls_one) INDEX 1.
DATA(ls_first) = lt_gr[ 1 ].              " 7.40 table expression
🪤 Trap: changing a work area does not change the table. You need MODIFY, or use a field symbol (L6). lt_gr[ 1 ] with no row → exception CX_SY_ITAB_LINE_NOT_FOUND.
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q8)

"A work area is a structure that holds one row of an internal table for reading or changing it; changes must be written back with MODIFY. Header lines are obsolete and not allowed in classes, so I use separate or inline work areas like INTO DATA(ls_gr), and field symbols when I need to change rows without copying."

🧠 Remember: work area = photocopy of one row. Change it → MODIFY back.

📒 Internal table types (Q3)

DATA lt_po TYPE HASHED TABLE OF ekko WITH UNIQUE KEY ebeln.
DATA lt_gr TYPE SORTED TABLE OF ty_gr WITH NON-UNIQUE KEY mblnr.
READ TABLE lt_po INTO DATA(ls_po) WITH TABLE KEY ebeln = lv_ebeln.
StandardSortedHashed
KeyNon-unique (default)Unique / non-uniqueUnique only
Read by keySlow (linear)Fast (binary)Fastest (constant)
INDEX accessYesYesNo
APPENDYesOnly if order staysNo (use INSERT)
Best forSmall tables, loops in orderLOOP … WHERE on key, rangesBig lookup tables
🚨 PDF fix: there is no SORTED BY addition. A sorted table uses its key. Standard + SORT + READ … BINARY SEARCH is the old trick.
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q3)

"Internal tables are in-memory tables. Standard tables keep insertion order and read by key linearly; sorted tables stay sorted by key and use binary search; hashed tables have a unique key and constant read time but no index. For example, in a matching loop a GR lookup on a big standard table is slow; a hashed table keyed on order and invoice number makes each read constant time."

🧠 Remember: notebook (standard) · dictionary (sorted) · phone contacts (hashed).
PART 3 · POINTERS

🏷️ Field symbols and data references (Q5)

📄 Work area: a photocopy of the row. Change = copy back.
🏷️ Field symbol: a nickname for the real row. Change = done. No copy, faster.
📮 Data reference: an address slip pointing to data. The data type can be decided at runtime.
" field symbol: change the real row, no MODIFY
LOOP AT lt_gr ASSIGNING FIELD-SYMBOL(<ls_gr>).
  <ls_gr>-menge = <ls_gr>-menge * 2.
ENDLOOP.
" data reference: create data of a type known only at runtime
DATA lr_any TYPE REF TO data.
CREATE DATA lr_any TYPE ('MSEG').
ASSIGN lr_any->* TO FIELD-SYMBOL(<ls_any>).   " ->* = the data behind the address
🏷️ Field symbol📮 Data reference
DeclareFIELD-SYMBOLS <fs> TYPE …DATA lr TYPE REF TO …
PointASSIGN / ASSIGNINGREF #( … ), CREATE DATA, REFERENCE INTO
UseDirectly <fs>-fieldDereference lr->* / lr->field
CheckIS ASSIGNEDIS BOUND
Can be stored in a tableNoYes
🪤 Trap: using a field symbol that points to nothing → dump GETWA_NOT_ASSIGNED. After READ TABLE … ASSIGNING, check sy-subrc or IS ASSIGNED.
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q5)

"A field symbol is a placeholder that points directly to existing data, so with LOOP ASSIGNING I change rows without copying or MODIFY. A data reference holds the address of data, can be created dynamically with CREATE DATA and dereferenced with ->*, and can be stored in tables. I use field symbols in big loops for speed, and data references for dynamic code like a generic file upload. An unassigned field symbol causes the dump GETWA_NOT_ASSIGNED, so I check IS ASSIGNED first."

🧠 Remember: photocopy (WA) · nickname (FS, ASSIGN) · address slip (REF, ->*).
PART 4 · DATABASE

🗄️ Transparent vs pooled vs cluster tables (Q4)

TypeHow it is storedExample (ECC)
🪟 Transparent1 SAP table = 1 DB table, same name and fieldsMARA, EKKO, MKPF, MSEG, your Y tables
🏊 PooledMany small SAP tables stored together in one DB table poolSmall customizing tables (e.g. in pool ATAB)
🧺 ClusterSeveral tables stored together in one DB table cluster, rows grouped by a common keyBSEG (cluster RFBLG), KONV (cluster KOCLU)
  • ❓ Why pooled / cluster? Old databases had limits on the number of tables and space. Saved space.
  • 😫 Problems: no JOINs, no secondary indexes, only Open SQL, slow for reports.
  • 🚀 Today: on HANA / S/4HANA they are converted to transparent tables (BSEG is transparent in S/4).
🚨 PDF fix: "BSEG is a cluster table" is true only for old ECC. In S/4HANA all are transparent.
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q4)

"A transparent table maps one-to-one to a database table. Pooled tables stored many small tables in one table pool, and cluster tables stored several tables together in a table cluster grouped by key, like BSEG in RFBLG in ECC; they saved space but couldn't be joined or indexed. On HANA and S/4HANA they are converted to transparent tables, so BSEG can be joined normally."

🧠 Remember: transparent = own flat 🏠 · pooled = PG room with many tenants 🛏️ · cluster = joint family by key 👨‍👩‍👧. S/4 = all own flats.

🎯 SELECT SINGLE vs UP TO 1 ROWS vs SELECT * (Q7)

" 1) full primary key known → SELECT SINGLE
SELECT SINGLE lifnr, bedat FROM ekko
  WHERE ebeln = @lv_ebeln INTO @DATA(ls_po).
" 2) many rows match, I want one (e.g. the latest) → UP TO 1 ROWS + ORDER BY
SELECT mblnr, budat FROM mkpf
  WHERE budat IN @s_budat ORDER BY budat DESCENDING
  INTO @DATA(ls_last) UP TO 1 ROWS.
ENDSELECT.
☝️ SELECT SINGLE🥇 UP TO 1 ROWS🚫 SELECT *
Use whenFull key known, one row existsMany may match, take one (with ORDER BY)Really need all columns (small tables)
ORDER BYNot allowedYes—
ResultOne row, the first foundOne row, the one you sorted forAll columns
CostCheapCheap with indexExpensive on big tables (Perf L3)

✅ After any SELECT check sy-subrc: 0 = found, 4 = nothing found.

🎤 INTERVIEW (Q7)

"SELECT SINGLE reads one row and is meant for the full primary key; without the full key the row it returns is not defined. SELECT UP TO 1 ROWS with ORDER BY is for picking one specific row when many match, like the latest GR. SELECT * reads all columns and should be avoided on big tables; I select only the fields I need and always check sy-subrc."

🧠 Remember: SINGLE = full key · UP TO 1 = many, pick one (ORDER BY) · * = avoid.

😂 Basics tricks

  • 🥭 Program types 1-M-F-K-J-I-S-T: "1 Mango For Kids, Juice In Steel Tumbler".
  • 🧰 RICEFW: "Rice Fry" 🍚 = Reports, Interfaces, Conversions, Enhancements, Forms, Workflows.
  • 📒 Notebook, dictionary, phone contacts = standard, sorted, hashed.
  • 🏷️ Photocopy, nickname, address slip = work area, field symbol, data reference.
  • 🏠 Own flat, PG room, joint family = transparent, pooled, cluster.
  • 🔢 sy-subrc 0 = "sab sahi", 4 = "kuch nahi mila" (nothing found).

🎮 Your turn (answer first, then tap)

🎤 FULL MODULE ANSWER

"ABAP is SAP's language for RICEFW objects, running on the application server with database-independent Open SQL. Program types like executable reports, module pools, function groups and class pools decide how code starts. Data lives in structures and internal tables: standard, sorted or hashed depending on access, with work areas or field symbols to process rows. Transparent tables map one-to-one to the database, while old pooled and cluster tables are transparent on S/4. I pick SELECT SINGLE for full keys, UP TO 1 ROWS with ORDER BY otherwise, and avoid SELECT *."

DOHands-on

  1. ADT class with IF_OO_ADT_CLASSRUN.
  2. Fill a standard and a hashed table with 1 lakh rows.
  3. Time 1,000 reads each (GET RUN TIME FIELD) and print both. Hashed should be far lower.
  4. Change rows once with a work area + MODIFY, once with ASSIGNING. Print to see both work.
  1. SE38: copy one of your reports. Find the lookup tables used inside loops.
  2. Change one to SORTED or HASHED. Work area + MODIFY → ASSIGNING.
  3. Compare runtime in SAT.
  4. SE11: open MSEG → Technical settings; open BSEG and check if it is transparent or cluster on your system.

TESTQuick check

1. Program type of an include?

I = include (never runs alone). J = interface pool. 1 = executable.

2. Which table type has a unique key only and no index?

Hashed = constant-time key reads, no INDEX access.

3. Does a DDIC structure store data in the database?

Transparent tables store rows; structures only define types.

4. You loop INTO a work area and change a field. The table…

Work area = copy. Field symbols change the real row.

5. Dump GETWA_NOT_ASSIGNED means…

Check sy-subrc or IS ASSIGNED before using it.

6. Many rows match, you want the latest. Use…

SELECT SINGLE can't sort and is for the full key.
Answer all 6 first.
⚡ Quick revise. Read top to bottom in 2 minutes. Forgot something? Switch to 📖 Learn.

🧱 Part 1 · ABAP & programs (Q1, Q2)

  • ABAP = SAP's language, app server, Open SQL, builds RICEFW ("Rice Fry").
  • Types 1 report · M module pool · F function group · K class pool · J interface pool · I include · S subroutine pool · T type group ("1 Mango For Kids, Juice In Steel Tumbler").

📦 Part 2 · Containers (Q3, Q6, Q8)

  • Structure = one row (DDIC: only shape). Internal table = many rows (DDIC table: stores data).
  • Work area = copy of one row → MODIFY back. Header lines obsolete. Inline INTO DATA(ls), lt[ 1 ].
  • Standard (linear, index) · sorted (binary, key, index) · hashed (unique key, constant, no index). No "SORTED BY".

🏷️ Part 3 · Pointers (Q5)

Field symbol = nickname (ASSIGNING, IS ASSIGNED, GETWA_NOT_ASSIGNED). Data ref = address (REF TO, CREATE DATA, ->*, IS BOUND).

🗄️ Part 4 · Database (Q4, Q7)

  • Transparent 1:1 · pooled (many small in one pool) · cluster (grouped by key, BSEG in ECC). S/4: all transparent.
  • SELECT SINGLE = full key · UP TO 1 ROWS + ORDER BY = pick one · SELECT * = avoid · sy-subrc 0 / 4.
🎤 30-SECOND ANSWER

"ABAP is SAP's language for RICEFW objects. I hold data in structures and internal tables, choosing standard, sorted or hashed by access pattern, and use field symbols to change rows without copies. Transparent tables map one-to-one to the database; pooled and cluster tables are transparent on S/4. I use SELECT SINGLE for full keys and UP TO 1 ROWS with ORDER BY otherwise."

MODULE 02 · PDF Q9–14 · COMPLETE ✅

Data Dictionary (DDIC)

One central place that describes all data: tables, fields, checks, F4 help, locks. T-codes: SE11, SE16N, SM30, SM12.

😫 PROBLEMSame field defined differently in many programs, wrong values typed in, two users overwriting each other's data.
💡 IDEADefine data once centrally, check values against a master list, show F4 help, lock a record while editing.
🔧 HOWDomain + data element, transparent tables, foreign keys, views, search helps, lock objects (SE11).
➡️ NEXTModule 03: Modularization. Later CDS views replace SE11 views (Module 10).
📦
Example for the whole module: our custom table ZGR_LOG that logs every 101 GR: client, material doc no., year, item, plant, status.

LEARNLevel by level

Part 1 · DDIC objects (Q9) Part 2 · Domain vs data element (Q10) Part 3 · Tables, keys, views (Q11, Q12) Part 4 · Search help (Q13) Part 5 · Lock objects (Q14)
PART 1 · DDIC

🤔 What is the Data Dictionary?

The ABAP Dictionary (DDIC, T-code SE11) is the central place where all data is described: which tables exist, which fields they have, what type and length each field is, which values are allowed, and what label users see.

  • ❓ Why? Without it, every program would define "material number = 18 characters" itself. With DDIC it's defined once; when SAP grew material to 40 characters in S/4HANA, it changed one place.
  • 🔁 Active everywhere: programs, screens and reports read the same definitions. Change a label once → every screen shows it.
  • 🗄️ Creates the DB tables: activate a table in SE11 → SAP creates it in the database.

🔍 Look at data: SE16N (or SE16) shows table contents. SE11 shows the definition.

🧠 Remember: DDIC = define data once (SE11). SE16N = see the data.

🧱 Key DDIC objects (Q9)

ObjectSimple meaningExample
🗄️ Database tableStores rows in the DBMSEG, ZGR_LOG
👁️ ViewA saved look at one or more tablesMaintenance view for SM30
🧬 DomainTechnical shape: type, length, allowed valuesCHAR 1, values O / C
🏷️ Data elementMeaning: labels, F1 help, uses a domain"GR log status"
📦 StructureA shape with fields, no data storedBAPI parameter structure
📚 Table typeType of an internal table (line type + table kind + key)ZTT_GR_LOG
🔎 Search helpThe F4 list of valuesPlant F4
🔒 Lock objectGenerates lock FMs to stop parallel editsEZGR_LOG
📦 Type group (old)Types and constants for programsSLIS
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q9)

"The ABAP Dictionary centrally defines data used across the system. Its key objects are database tables, views, domains for technical attributes, data elements for semantic meaning and labels, structures, table types, search helps for F4 and lock objects. In my project the staging tables, their structures and table types were all defined in SE11 and reused by the import and matching programs."

🧠 Remember: "TV DD STS L" = Table, View, Domain, Data element, Structure, Table type, Search help, Lock object.
PART 2 · DOMAIN VS DATA ELEMENT

🧬 Domain = shape · 🏷️ Data element = meaning (Q10)

🧬 Domain ZD_STATUS: CHAR 1, values O / C→🏷️ Data element ZDE_GR_STATUS: label "GR status"→🗄️ Field STATUS in ZGR_LOG
🧬 Domain🏷️ Data element
Answers"What does it look like?""What does it mean?"
HoldsData type, length, decimals, fixed values / value range, conversion routineField labels (short, medium, long, heading), F1 documentation, search help, the domain
ReuseOne domain → many data elementsOne data element → many table fields
👥
Classic example: sold-to and ship-to customer share one shape (domain KUNNR, CHAR 10) but need different labels ("Sold-to party", "Ship-to party"), so two data elements.

🔤 Conversion routine (in the domain), e.g. ALPHA: adds leading zeros (4711 → 0000004711). That's why SELECTs with numbers sometimes find nothing: the value must be in internal format (CONVERSION_EXIT_ALPHA_INPUT).

🎤 INTERVIEW (Q10)

"A domain defines the technical attributes: data type, length, decimals, fixed values and conversion routine. A data element gives the business meaning: field labels, F1 documentation and search help, and it refers to a domain. One domain can serve many data elements; for example KUNNR is one domain for sold-to and ship-to. For example, for a GR log table I would create domain ZD_STATUS with fixed values and data element ZDE_GR_STATUS with labels. A common issue: a SELECT on vendor numbers finds nothing because of the ALPHA conversion, so the input must be converted first."

🧠 Remember: domain = shape (kapde ka size), data element = meaning (naam ka tag) 👕🏷️.
PART 3 · TABLES

🗄️ Build a table in SE11

  1. SE11 → Database table ZGR_LOG → Create → short text.
  2. Delivery and maintenance tab: delivery class (A = application / business data, C = customizing) and Data Browser / Table View Maint. (allow SE16 / SM30?).
  3. Fields: MANDT (key, client) → MBLNR, MJAHR, ZEILE (keys) → WERKS → STATUS (data element ZDE_GR_STATUS).
  4. Technical settings: data class (APPL0 master, APPL1 transaction, APPL2 customizing), size category, buffering (Perf tab, L6).
  5. Enhancement category (Extras): can others append fields later?
  6. Activate → SAP creates the table in the database.
  • 🏢 MANDT first in the key = client-dependent: each client sees only its own rows.
  • 🛠️ Table Maintenance Generator (SE11 → Utilities → TMG, or SE54) creates screens so users can edit entries in SM30.
  • ➕ Append structure: add custom fields to an SAP table without modifying it.
🧠 Remember: delivery class → fields (MANDT + keys) → technical settings → enhancement category → activate → TMG for SM30.

🔗 Foreign key and check table (Q11)

A foreign key links a field to a check table: the value must exist there. Example: ZGR_LOG-WERKS must exist in T001W (plants).

Foreign key table ZGR_LOG (field WERKS)→ checks →Check table T001W (key WERKS)
  • ✅ User types a plant on a screen → SAP checks T001W → error if it doesn't exist.
  • 🔎 You also get F4 values from the check table for free.
  • 🔢 Cardinality (e.g. 1:CN): one plant can have many log rows; each log row has exactly one plant.
  • 🧩 Other examples: EKPO-EBELN → EKKO; MSEG-MATNR → MARA.
⚠️ Interview detail: the database does not enforce SAP foreign keys. Only screens check them. A program doing INSERT directly is not stopped, so programs must validate themselves (like your import programs did).
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q11)

"A foreign key links a field to a check table so only existing values are accepted, for example a plant field checked against T001W, with a cardinality like 1:CN, and it also provides F4 help. The check runs on screens, not in the database, so a direct INSERT isn't stopped. In my project, data came from files, not screens, so our import programs validated plants and vendors against the master tables themselves."

🧠 Remember: foreign key = "value must exist in the check table". Screen checks it, DB doesn't.

🆚 Table vs view (Q12)

🗄️ Table👁️ View
Stores data?YesNo: shows data of other tables
Built fromFieldsOne or more tables + join conditions + selected fields
UseKeep dataRead joined data easily, maintain several tables, F4

👁️ 4 classic SE11 view types

ViewDoesToday
Database viewInner join of tables, read in SELECTCDS view
Projection viewOne table, hides some fieldsCDS view
Maintenance viewEdit related tables together in SM30Still used on-prem
Help viewOuter join, used inside search helpsCDS value help
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q12)

"A table physically stores data, while a view stores no data and presents fields from one or more tables through join conditions. SE11 has database views for inner joins, projection views to hide fields, maintenance views to edit related tables in SM30, and help views for search helps. Today I'd model joins as CDS views, which add calculations, associations and annotations."

🧠 Remember: table = godown (stores), view = window (shows). DB, Projection, Maintenance, Help = "DPMH".
PART 4 · SEARCH HELP

🔎 Search helps (Q13)

A search help is the F4 popup: the list of possible values when the user presses F4 on a field.

TypeWhat it isExample
🔹 ElementaryOne way to search: one selection method (table / view), its parametersFind plant by name
🔸 CollectiveSeveral elementary search helps shown as tabsFind a material by description, by plant, by class…

🛠️ Create an elementary search help

  1. SE11 → Search help ZSH_PLANT → Elementary.
  2. Selection method: T001W (or a help view).
  3. Parameters: WERKS (IMP + EXP = goes in and out), NAME1 (only shown). Set list positions.
  4. Dialog type: show values immediately, or first a restriction popup.
  5. Attach it: to a table field (SE11 → field → Srch Help), to a data element, or to the check table.

🧩 Search help exit: an FM (copy of F4IF_SHLP_EXIT_EXAMPLE) to change the list with code, e.g. only show my plants.

🎤 INTERVIEW (Q13)

"A search help provides the F4 list of values. An elementary search help has one selection method, like a table or help view, with import and export parameters; a collective search help groups several elementary ones as tabs. I create them in SE11 and attach them to a table field, a data element or the check table. For example, for a plant field I would build an elementary help on T001W and use a search help exit to restrict it to the user's plants."

🧠 Remember: elementary = one road, collective = many roads as tabs. Exit = custom code.
PART 5 · LOCKS

🔒 Lock objects (Q14)

Two users edit the same GR log row → the second save overwrites the first. 😱 SAP keeps a lock table in memory (the enqueue server) of who locked what.

🛠️ Create one

  1. SE11 → Lock object → name must start with E: EZGR_LOG.
  2. Primary table ZGR_LOG, lock mode (e.g. E), lock parameters = key fields (MBLNR, MJAHR, ZEILE).
  3. Activate → SAP generates 2 FMs: ENQUEUE_EZGR_LOG and DEQUEUE_EZGR_LOG.
CALL FUNCTION 'ENQUEUE_EZGR_LOG'
  EXPORTING  mblnr = lv_mblnr  mjahr = lv_mjahr
  EXCEPTIONS foreign_lock = 1  system_failure = 2  OTHERS = 3.
IF sy-subrc <> 0.
  MESSAGE |Locked by { sy-msgv1 }| TYPE 'E'.   " who has it
ENDIF.
" ... read, change, UPDATE zgr_log ...
CALL FUNCTION 'DEQUEUE_EZGR_LOG'
  EXPORTING mblnr = lv_mblnr  mjahr = lv_mjahr.
ModeMeaning
EExclusive, cumulative: write lock (same user can lock again)
XExclusive, non-cumulative: even the same user can't lock twice
SShared: many can read, nobody can write
OOptimistic: becomes exclusive only when changing

👀 Live locks: SM12 (and delete stuck ones carefully). Locks are also released at program end / COMMIT, depending on _SCOPE.

🪤 Trap: SAP locks are logical: they only work if every program calls ENQUEUE. A program that just does UPDATE is not stopped.
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q14)

"A lock object stops two users changing the same data at once. I create it in SE11 with a name starting with E, the primary table, lock mode and key fields; activation generates ENQUEUE and DEQUEUE function modules. I call ENQUEUE before changing, handle FOREIGN_LOCK to tell the user who holds it, and DEQUEUE afterwards. Modes are E, X, S and O, and locks are visible in SM12. For example, on a GR log table a lock object stops two support users reprocessing the same record in parallel."

🧠 Remember: E-name → ENQUEUE / DEQUEUE → modes E X S O → SM12. Logical lock, everyone must call it.

😂 DDIC tricks

  • 👕🏷️ Domain = kapde ka size, data element = naam ka tag.
  • 🧱 Objects "TV DD STS L": Table, View, Domain, Data element, Structure, Table type, Search help, Lock object.
  • 🏢 MANDT first = "har client ka apna ghar" (client-dependent).
  • 🪟 Table = godown, view = window. 4 views = "DPMH" (DB, Projection, Maintenance, Help).
  • 🛣️ Search help: elementary = one road, collective = many roads.
  • 🔒 Lock object starts with E = "Enqueue". SM12 = "Show Me who locked".
  • 🔢 ALPHA = "add leading zeros": 4711 → 0000004711.

🎮 Your turn (answer first, then tap)

🎤 FULL MODULE ANSWER

"The ABAP Dictionary centrally defines tables, views, domains, data elements, structures, table types, search helps and lock objects. Domains hold the technical shape and fixed values, data elements the meaning and labels. Tables have a delivery class, MANDT for client dependency, technical settings and foreign keys to check tables, which screens enforce. Views join or project tables, search helps give F4 values, and lock objects generate ENQUEUE and DEQUEUE function modules, visible in SM12. My project's staging tables were built with these pieces."

DOHands-on

  1. ADT: domain ZD_STATUS (CHAR 1, fixed values O / C).
  2. Data element ZDE_GR_STATUS with labels.
  3. Database table ZGR_LOG (client, mblnr, mjahr, zeile, werks, status). Activate, fill rows from a console class, F8 preview.
  4. Lock object EZGR_LOG on it. Call ENQUEUE / DEQUEUE from the class.
  1. SE11: same domain, data element and table ZGR_LOG.
  2. Foreign key on WERKS → check table T001W; test F4.
  3. Table Maintenance Generator → maintain entries in SM30.
  4. Elementary search help on T001W, attach to the field.
  5. Lock object, call ENQUEUE in two sessions, watch SM12.

TESTQuick check

Domain = shape (type, length, values). Data element = meaning.

2. MANDT as first key field means the table is…

Each client sees only its own rows.

3. Who checks foreign keys?

Direct INSERTs are not stopped.

4. Which view lets users maintain related tables in SM30?

Plus the Table Maintenance Generator.

5. A search help that shows several search tabs is…

Collective = many elementary search helps as tabs.

See live locks in SM12.

📚 Part 1 · Objects (Q9)

DDIC (SE11) defines data once. Objects "TV DD STS L": table, view, domain, data element, structure, table type, search help, lock object (+ type group). SE16N shows data.

🧬 Part 2 · Domain vs data element (Q10)

Domain = type, length, fixed values, conversion routine (ALPHA). Data element = labels, F1, search help, refers to a domain. One domain → many elements (KUNNR: sold-to, ship-to).

🗄️ Part 3 · Tables, keys, views (Q11, Q12)

  • Table: delivery class (A / C), MANDT key, technical settings (data class, size, buffering), enhancement category, TMG → SM30, append structures.
  • Foreign key → check table (WERKS → T001W), cardinality, F4. Checked by screens, not DB.
  • View stores no data. Database (inner join), projection, maintenance (SM30), help (outer join). Today CDS.

🔎 Part 4 · Search help (Q13)

Elementary (one selection method, IMP / EXP params, dialog type) vs collective (tabs). Attach to field, data element or check table. Exit FM for custom logic.

🔒 Part 5 · Locks (Q14)

SE11 lock object E… → ENQUEUE_ / DEQUEUE_ FMs · FOREIGN_LOCK · modes E (write), X (exclusive), S (shared), O (optimistic) · SM12 · logical, every program must call it.

🎤 30-SECOND ANSWER

"The ABAP Dictionary defines data centrally: domains for technical shape, data elements for meaning, tables with client dependency and foreign keys, views, search helps for F4, and lock objects that generate ENQUEUE and DEQUEUE function modules. For example, a staging table with a check table for plants and a lock object to stop parallel reprocessing."

MODULE 03 · PDF Q15–18 · COMPLETE ✅

Modularization

Write code once, give it a name, call that name many times. T-codes: SE38, SE37, SE24, SE80.

😫 PROBLEMSame SELECT on MSEG copy-pasted in 4 reports. One change = 4 fixes, and you forget one.
💡 IDEAMake one block of code and give it a name. Call that same name again and again.
🔧 HOWInclude, FORM, Function Module (SE37), Method (SE24), Macro.
➡️ NEXTModule 4: Reports & ALV (the field catalog from L13 comes back).

LEARNLevel by level

Part 1 · Big idea (Q15) Part 2 · Subroutines Part 3 · Function Modules (Q16) Part 4 · Function Group (Q17) Part 5 · Macros (Q18)
PART 1 · THE BIG IDEA

🤔 What is modularization?

Think of your GR report. Somewhere in it there are 5 lines that read the GR quantity for a PO from MSEG.

Instead of writing those 5 lines again every time you need them, you:

  1. Write them one time.
  2. Put them in a block and give the block a name, like get_gr.
  3. Wherever you need it, just write the name. SAP runs the block for you.
1 block: get_gr→call 1·call 2·call 3

🚶 What happens when you call it?

Main code runs→PERFORM get_gr→SAP jumps into the block→runs the SELECT→ENDFORM→comes back to the next line

❓ Why does it exist?

  • 🔁 Reuse: the same logic is needed in many places (many reports, many screens).
  • 👀 Easy to read: the main program becomes a short list of names: get_po, get_gr, show_alv. You understand it in 10 seconds.
  • 🛠️ One fix: change the block once, every caller gets the fix.
  • 🐞 Easy debug: a bug in GR reading? Look only inside get_gr.
🧠 Remember: one block, one name, call it many times.

😫 Before: copy-paste

Two reports, ZGR_DAILY and ZGR_VENDOR, both had the same code typed in:

" in ZGR_DAILY ... and again in ZGR_VENDOR
SELECT mblnr, menge FROM mseg
  WHERE ebeln = @p_ebeln AND bwart = '101'
  INTO TABLE @DATA(lt_gr).

🔑 MSEG = GR item table · EBELN = PO number · BWART = movement type · 101 = GR for a PO · MBLNR = material document number · MENGE = quantity · @ = "this is an ABAP variable".

💥
Real pain: the user says "also skip reversed GRs (102)". With copy-paste you must fix 4 reports. You forget one, and that one shows wrong numbers. Nobody notices for a month. 😱

💡 After: one block, many calls

FORM get_gr USING pv_ebeln TYPE ebeln.
  SELECT mblnr, menge FROM mseg
    WHERE ebeln = @pv_ebeln AND bwart = '101'
    INTO TABLE @gt_gr.
ENDFORM.

PERFORM get_gr USING p_ebeln.  " call it anywhere

🔑 FORM … ENDFORM = the block (start and end). get_gr = its name. PERFORM = "go and run this block". USING = the value you send in (here the PO number).

😩 Copy-paste✅ Modular
Bug fix4 places1 place
Read code2,000 linesShort names
DebugHunt everywhereF5 jumps into one block
New reportCopy againJust call the name
💡 MSEG = Materialbeleg SEGment = GR item table. Header = MKPF (K = Kopf). S/4: MATDOC.
♻️ Reuse in your project: "read GR for a PO" becomes one block. The GR report, the PO report and the ALV report (Module 4) all call it.
🧠 Remember: copy-paste = many fixes, modular = one fix.

🧰 The 5 techniques (Q15)

ABAP gives you 5 ways to make a named block. Each one is for a different size of job.

TechniqueKeywordWhereIn simple words
📄 IncludeINCLUDESE38Cuts a long program into files. Not called, just pasted in
🔁 SubroutinePERFORMSE38Small block inside one program
🧰 Function ModuleCALL FUNCTIONSE37Block stored centrally. Any program can call it, even another system (RFC)
🏛️ Methodobj->m( )SE24 / ADTBlock inside a class. The modern way ⭐
⚡ MacroDEFINESE38Text shortcut. SAP swaps it for code before compiling. You can't debug inside it

📡 RFC = Remote Function Call: one system calls an FM in another system.

🧭 Which one when?

Your needOld ECC wayModern way
Reuse inside one programFORMLocal class method
Reuse in many programsFunction ModuleGlobal class (SE24)
Called from another systemRFC Function ModuleRFC FM / OData
Organize a huge programIncludesClasses

🧪 Real FM for GR: BAPI_GOODSMVT_CREATE posts a goods movement (like MIGO 101) from code. BAPI = an SAP-released FM that is safe to call.

🧠 Remember: I Sub Fun Met Mac. Only methods are modern.

📄 Include: what is it?

An Include is a piece of code saved as its own object (program type I). When the main program is compiled, SAP pastes the code in at the INCLUDE line. It is like the code was typed there.

  • ❓ Why? A 3,000-line report is hard to read. Split it into files by job: data here, screen there, FORMs there.
  • 🕰️ Before: data, screen, logic and FORMs all in one long program. Scroll, scroll, scroll.
  • 🚫 Not a call: no input, no output, no PERFORM. Only copy-paste by the system.

🔧 How a big GR report is split

REPORT zgr_report.
INCLUDE zgr_report_top.  " TYPES, DATA (globals)
INCLUDE zgr_report_sel.  " PARAMETERS p_ebeln
INCLUDE zgr_report_f01.  " all FORM ... ENDFORM

START-OF-SELECTION.
  PERFORM get_gr USING p_ebeln.

🔑 TOP = declarations (top of program). SEL = selection screen. F01 = FORM routines. START-OF-SELECTION = main logic starts after you press F8.

SuffixHoldsSeen in
TOPGlobal DATA, TYPESReports, module pools
O01PBO modules (before screen shows)Module pools
I01PAI modules (after user clicks)Module pools
F01FORM routinesBoth

🖥️ Module pool = screen program (type M). PBO = Process Before Output. PAI = Process After Input.

🛠️ Create one

  1. SE38: type INCLUDE zgr_report_top. in your report.
  2. Double-click the name → "Create?" → Yes.
  3. Write your DATA there, save, activate.
  4. SE80 → your program → see all includes in the tree.

🆚 Include vs Subroutine

📄 Include🔁 FORM
How it worksCode pasted at compile timeCalled at runtime
Input / outputNoneUSING / CHANGING
Runs alone (F8)?NoNo, needs a program
GoalOrganize codeReuse logic
⚠️ Careful: one include used in 3 programs → you change it once, all 3 change. Check where-used (Ctrl+Shift+F3) first. Includes don't exist in ABAP Cloud.
🧠 Remember: Include = photocopy pasted in. It organizes, it does not get called.

🆚 Old way vs new way

🕰️ ECC classic🚀 Modern / S/4
Main toolFORM + FMClass methods
FORM, MacroUsed a lotObsolete
ABAP Cloud (BTP)—Classes. FORM not allowed
Errorssy-subrcExceptions

🤔 Why did SAP move away from FORM?

  • 🌍 FORMs often read and change global data. One FORM can break another without you seeing it.
  • 🧪 Hard to test alone. Classes can have unit tests (ABAP Unit).
  • 🚨 No clean error handling. Methods raise exceptions that the caller must handle.
  • 🔒 A class has a clear "door" (public methods). Inside stays private.
⚠️ Honest: FORM and Macro still run on-prem, and old projects are full of them, so you must read them. But SAP marks them obsolete, and in ABAP Cloud (BTP) you write classes: FORM is not allowed there.
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q15)

"Modularization means splitting code into reusable blocks. ABAP has includes, subroutines, function modules in SE37, class methods in SE24, and macros. In my GR report I moved the MSEG read for movement type 101 into one block so all reports used the same logic. Earlier the SELECT was copy-pasted and a bug fix was missed in one place; after modularizing it was one fix. Today I prefer methods, because FORM and macros are obsolete and ABAP Cloud is built on classes."

🧠 Remember: can read FORM, should write methods.

😂 Tricks you won't forget

  • 🍔 "I Sub Fun Met Mac" → "Main Sub-Fun mein Mac se Met kiya": Include, Subroutine, Function module, Method, Macro.
  • 🔢 SE37 vs SE24: 3+7 = 10 → old (FM). 2+4 = 6 → class is "six"-y and modern 😎.
  • 📄 Include = photocopy: SAP pastes it in. 📞 FORM = phone call: you call it, it answers.
  • ☁️ BTP rule: "Cloud mein sirf class chalegi" → write classes in ABAP Cloud. FORM not allowed.
🪤 Trap Qs: Is an Include called at runtime? No, merged at compile time. Can you write FORM in BTP? No.

🎮 Your turn (answer first, then tap)

PART 2 · SUBROUTINES

🤔 What is it?

A FORM can take values in and give values back. So the same block works for any PO: today 4500001234, tomorrow 4500005678.

  • ❓ Why? A block with no input works for one fixed PO only. Useless.
  • 🕰️ Before: the FORM read global variables directly (like p_ebeln from the screen). Hidden link: rename the variable and the FORM breaks.
  • 💡 Now: send the PO in, get the total back. Anyone reading PERFORM sees exactly what goes in and what comes out.
DATA gv_total TYPE menge_d.
PERFORM get_total USING    p_ebeln
                  CHANGING gv_total.

FORM get_total USING    pv_ebeln TYPE ebeln
               CHANGING cv_total TYPE menge_d.
  SELECT SUM( menge ) FROM mseg
    WHERE ebeln = @pv_ebeln AND bwart = '101'
    INTO @cv_total.
ENDFORM.

🚶 Step by step

  1. PERFORM sends p_ebeln (say 4500001234) into the FORM.
  2. Inside, it is called pv_ebeln. Same value, new name.
  3. The SELECT adds all 101 quantities for that PO into cv_total.
  4. At ENDFORM, cv_total is the caller's gv_total. Back in the main code, gv_total holds the total.

🔑 MENGE_D = SAP data element for quantity. SUM( menge ) = the database adds the numbers. Name style: pv_ = parameter value, cv_ = changing value, gv_ = global variable.

WordDirectionUse for
USING➡️ inValues the FORM reads
CHANGING⬅️ backValues the FORM fills
TABLES⬅️➡️ tableOld way to pass internal tables. Obsolete: use USING/CHANGING with a table type
🪤 Trap: values are matched by position, not by name. 1st in PERFORM → 1st in FORM. Names can differ (p_ebeln → pv_ebeln).

📛 Actual parameter = what you pass in PERFORM. Formal parameter = the name inside FORM.

🧠 Remember: USING = in, CHANGING = back, matched by position.

🔗 By reference vs 📄 by value

When you pass a variable, SAP gives the FORM either the original variable (by reference) or a copy of it (by value).

DATA gv_qty TYPE i VALUE 10.
PERFORM add_ref USING gv_qty.  " gv_qty = 11 !
PERFORM add_val USING gv_qty.  " gv_qty stays 11

FORM add_ref USING pv_qty TYPE i.
  pv_qty = pv_qty + 1.
ENDFORM.
FORM add_val USING VALUE(pv_qty) TYPE i.
  pv_qty = pv_qty + 1.
ENDFORM.

🚶 Step by step

  1. Start: gv_qty = 10.
  2. add_ref: pv_qty is gv_qty (same memory). +1 → gv_qty = 11.
  3. add_val: pv_qty is a copy (11). +1 → copy = 12. Copy is thrown away at ENDFORM.
  4. End: gv_qty = 11.
WaySyntaxChange inside seen outside?
🔗 Reference (default)USING p / CHANGING pYes, at once. No copy, fast
📄 ValueUSING VALUE(p)No. Works on a copy
📄➡️ Value and resultCHANGING VALUE(p)Yes, but only at ENDFORM (not if it stops with an error)
🪤 Big trap: USING without VALUE is still by reference. If you change it inside, the caller's variable changes too. Use VALUE( ) to protect it.

🏠 Local data

DATA inside a FORM is local: born at FORM, gone at ENDFORM. Other FORMs can't see it. STATICS keeps its value between calls (like a counter).

🎤 INTERVIEW

"A subroutine is a FORM block called with PERFORM inside the same program. I pass inputs with USING and get results back with CHANGING; TABLES is obsolete. By default parameters are passed by reference, so I use VALUE( ) when the FORM must not change the caller's data. In my GR report one FORM took the PO number and returned the total 101 quantity. Once a USING parameter was changed inside the FORM and the caller's PO number got overwritten, so I switched it to VALUE( )."

🧠 Remember: default = reference (original). VALUE( ) = copy (safe).

😂 FORM tricks

  • ➡️ USING = "Use karo": only read it. ⬅️ CHANGING = "Change karke wapas do": fill it and send back.
  • 👫 Jodi rule: PERFORM and FORM stand in the same line order. Names can differ, position cannot.
  • 🔗 Reference = shared Google Doc: you edit, everyone sees. 📄 VALUE( ) = PDF copy: your edits stay with you.
  • 🗑️ TABLES = dadaji's style. Still runs, but don't write new code with it.

🎮 Your turn (answer first, then tap)

🏢 Try in office SE38: run the add_ref / add_val code, press F5 into each FORM, watch gv_qty in the debugger.
PART 3 · FUNCTION MODULES

🤔 What is a Function Module (FM)?

A FORM lives inside one program. Other programs can't use it nicely.

A Function Module is a named block stored centrally in the Function Builder (SE37). Any program in the system can call it by its name. Even another SAP system can call it (RFC).

  • ❓ Why? Your GR total is needed in 10 different reports. Write it once as an FM, all 10 call it.
  • 🕰️ Before: copy the FORM into every report, or call it with PERFORM … IN PROGRAM zother. Fragile: rename the other program and everything breaks.
  • 💡 Now: one FM, one name, the whole system can use it.

🧱 The FM (SE37)

FUNCTION z_get_gr_total.
*" IMPORTING  iv_ebeln TYPE ebeln
*" EXPORTING  ev_total TYPE menge_d
*" EXCEPTIONS no_gr
  SELECT SUM( menge ) FROM mseg
    WHERE ebeln = @iv_ebeln AND bwart = '101'
    INTO @ev_total.
  IF ev_total IS INITIAL. RAISE no_gr. ENDIF.
ENDFUNCTION.

🔑 The *" lines are written by SAP from the tabs you fill in SE37. iv_ = import value, ev_ = export value. RAISE no_gr = "stop and report the error no_gr". IS INITIAL = empty / zero.

📞 Calling it from your report

CALL FUNCTION 'Z_GET_GR_TOTAL'
  EXPORTING  iv_ebeln = p_ebeln
  IMPORTING  ev_total = gv_total
  EXCEPTIONS no_gr    = 1
             OTHERS   = 2.
IF sy-subrc <> 0.
  MESSAGE 'No GR for this PO' TYPE 'I'.
ENDIF.

🔑 FM name in quotes and CAPITALS. sy-subrc = return code: 0 = OK, 1 = no_gr was raised, 2 = any other error. TYPE 'I' = info popup.

🪤 Mirror trap: the caller's EXPORTING = the FM's IMPORTING. You send out what the FM takes in. Like a mirror, everything is flipped.

🗂️ SE37 tabs

TabWhat you put there
AttributesFunction group, type: normal / remote-enabled (RFC) / update module
ImportValues coming in (iv_ebeln)
ExportValues going back (ev_total)
ChangingCome in and go back changed
TablesInternal tables (old style, obsolete)
ExceptionsError names like no_gr
Source codeYour logic
💡 Shortcut: in SE38 press Pattern (Ctrl+F6) → type the FM name → SAP writes the whole CALL FUNCTION block for you.
⚠️ Typo in the name? Syntax check can't see inside quotes. At runtime you get dump CALL_FUNCTION_NOT_FOUND (check in ST22).
🧠 Remember: FM = central block in SE37. Caller EXPORTING = FM IMPORTING.

🆚 FORM vs Function Module (Q16)

🔁 FORM🧰 Function Module
Lives inInside one programSE37, inside a function group
Who can callSame programAny program, other systems (RFC)
CallPERFORM nameCALL FUNCTION 'NAME'
Params matched byPositionName (iv_ebeln = …)
ParamsUSING, CHANGING, TABLESIMPORTING, EXPORTING, CHANGING, TABLES, EXCEPTIONS
Optional paramsNoYes, with default values
Error handlingNothing built inEXCEPTIONS → sy-subrc
Test aloneNoYes: SE37 → F8
Remote / update taskNoYes: RFC, IN UPDATE TASK
TodayObsoleteStill used (BAPIs), new code → classes

🧪 Why "test alone" is a big deal

  1. SE37 → Z_GET_GR_TOTAL → F8.
  2. Type a PO number in IV_EBELN → F8.
  3. See EV_TOTAL right away. No report needed!

A FORM can only be tested by running the whole program.

📡 RFC (remote-enabled) = another system calls your FM over the network. 🧾 Update module = runs in the update task to save data safely after COMMIT WORK (Module 12, LUW).

🎤 INTERVIEW (Q16)

"A subroutine is a FORM inside one program, called with PERFORM, with parameters matched by position. A function module is created in SE37 inside a function group and can be called from any program, even remotely through RFC. FMs have named import, export, changing and tables parameters plus exceptions, and can be tested alone in SE37. In my project the GR total was needed in several reports, so I moved it from a FORM into Z_GET_GR_TOTAL with an exception NO_GR. A typo in the FM name once caused a CALL_FUNCTION_NOT_FOUND dump, so now I always insert calls with the Pattern button."

🧠 Remember: FORM = one program, by position. FM = whole system, by name, testable in SE37.
PART 4 · FUNCTION GROUP

🤔 What is a Function Group? (Q17)

A Function Group is a container that holds related FMs. Every FM must belong to exactly one function group. You create the group first, then the FMs inside it.

  • ❓ Why? FMs that do related work (all your GR helpers) sit together and can share global data.
  • 🧠 Memory: when a program calls the first FM of a group, SAP loads the whole group into memory. Its global data stays alive while that program runs.
  • 🗂️ Order: one place to find all GR FMs, one transport, one owner.

🏗️ What SAP creates for group ZFG_GR

ObjectNameHolds
Main programSAPLZFG_GRJust a list of includes (type F)
Global dataLZFG_GRTOPDATA shared by all FMs in the group
FM listLZFG_GRUXXSAP-managed, never edit
FM 1, FM 2 …LZFG_GRU01, U02Code of each FM
Your FORMsLZFG_GRF01Helper FORMs (optional)

🔤 Name rule: SAPL + group = main program. L + group + TOP / UXX / U01 = includes. Same TOP idea as in L4.

🔁 Shared data example

" LZFG_GRTOP
DATA gv_last_ebeln TYPE ebeln.

" FM Z_GR_SET_PO stores it
gv_last_ebeln = iv_ebeln.
" FM Z_GR_GET_TOTAL (same group) can read it
SELECT SUM( menge ) FROM mseg
  WHERE ebeln = @gv_last_ebeln INTO @ev_total.
🪤 Trap: global data in TOP stays between calls in the same program run. Old values can leak into the next call. Clear them when you start.

🛠️ Create it (office)

  1. SE80 → Function group → ZFG_GR → Create (or SE37 → Goto → Function groups → Create).
  2. Give a short text, save in your package and transport.
  3. Right-click the group → Activate (the main program must be active).
  4. SE37 → create Z_GET_GR_TOTAL, Attributes tab → group ZFG_GR.
🔍 Any FM: SE37 → Attributes tab shows its function group. Try BAPI_GOODSMVT_CREATE.
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q17)

"A function group is a container program of type F for related function modules; every FM must belong to one. SAP creates the main program SAPL plus the group name, with a TOP include for global data shared by all its FMs, and one include per FM. When the first FM is called, the whole group is loaded and its global data lives for that program run. I created ZFG_GR for our GR helper FMs. Once old values in the TOP include leaked into the next call, so I cleared the global data at the start of each FM."

🧠 Remember: Function group = house 🏠. FMs = rooms. TOP = shared kitchen. House name = SAPL + group.
PART 5 · MACROS

🤔 What is a Macro?

A Macro is a text shortcut. You write a few lines once with DEFINE. Every time you use the macro name, SAP replaces it with those lines before compiling. Like Find & Replace.

  • ❓ Why? Old code used it for very short lines repeated many times, like adding ALV columns.
  • 🔢 Placeholders &1 to &9 are filled with the values you write after the name. Max 9.
DEFINE add_fcat.
  ls_fcat-fieldname = &1.
  ls_fcat-seltext_m = &2.
  APPEND ls_fcat TO lt_fcat.
END-OF-DEFINITION.

add_fcat 'MBLNR' 'GR doc'.
add_fcat 'MENGE' 'Quantity'.

🔑 lt_fcat = field catalog: the list of ALV columns (Module 4). fieldname = which field, seltext_m = medium column heading. &1 becomes 'MBLNR', &2 becomes 'GR doc'.

🔍 What SAP really compiles

ls_fcat-fieldname = 'MBLNR'.
ls_fcat-seltext_m = 'GR doc'.
APPEND ls_fcat TO lt_fcat.
" ... and the same 3 lines again for MENGE

📏 Rules

  • 📍 Must be defined before its first use (top of the program).
  • 🏠 Works only inside that program.
  • 🐞 Debugger can't step inside: the whole macro runs as one line.
  • 🚫 No types on &1, no local data, no checks. Errors are hard to find.
🧠 Remember: Macro = Find & Replace before compile. &1–&9, no debug.

🆚 Macro vs Subroutine (Q18)

⚡ Macro🔁 FORM
What happensText replaced at compile timeReal block called at runtime
Parameters&1–&9, no typesUSING / CHANGING with TYPE
DebugCan't step insideF5 into it
Where definedBefore first useAnywhere in the program
Local dataNoYes
Size2–5 short linesAny size
StatusObsolete, avoidObsolete, but very common in old code
Modern wayMethod or a small loopMethod
🪤 Traps: "Is a macro called at runtime?" No, it is replaced before compile. "Is a macro faster?" No real gain. It only saves typing, and costs readability.
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q18)

"A macro is defined with DEFINE and END-OF-DEFINITION, takes placeholders &1 to &9, and is replaced by its code at compile time, so you can't debug inside it and there are no type checks. A subroutine is a real FORM called at runtime with typed USING and CHANGING parameters and local data. In an old ALV report I found a macro that filled the field catalog. It hid a wrong field name that the debugger couldn't show, so I replaced it with a small method and the bug was easy to find."

🧠 Remember: macro = compile-time text, FORM = runtime block.

😂 Parts 3–5 tricks

  • 🪞 Mirror rule: "Caller ka EXPORTING = FM ka IMPORTING". Sab ulta.
  • 🔢 SE37: "3 + 7 = 10 rooms in the FM house". House name = SAPL + group (say it as "SAP Library").
  • 🏠 Function group = joint family: rooms (FMs) share one kitchen (TOP). Food left in the kitchen is still there next time, so clean it! 🍲
  • 🔎 Macro = Ctrl+H before compile. &1–&9 = only 9 seats in the macro bus 🚌.
  • 🧪 FM superpower: test alone with F8 in SE37. FORM can't.

🎮 Your turn (answer first, then tap)

🎤 FULL MODULE ANSWER

"ABAP code can be modularized with includes to organize code, FORM subroutines inside one program, function modules in SE37 inside function groups for system-wide and remote use, class methods in SE24, and macros as compile-time text. In my GR work I moved repeated MSEG logic into a FORM, then into an FM so several reports could share it, tested it alone in SE37 and handled errors with exceptions. For new code I use class methods, because FORM and macros are obsolete and ABAP Cloud is built on classes."

DOHands-on

  1. ADT: new class ZCL_GR_DEMO, add interface IF_OO_ADT_CLASSRUN.
  2. Write method get_double( iv_qty ) returning iv_qty * 2.
  3. In main, call it 3 times with out->write( ).
  4. F9 → console shows 3 results from one method.
  1. SE38: report ZGR_MOD_DEMO with PARAMETERS p_ebeln TYPE ebeln.
  2. Put the MSEG 101 SELECT in FORM get_gr; call with PERFORM.
  3. Debug: F5 jumps into the FORM.
  4. SE37 → BAPI_GOODSMVT_CREATE → Attributes tab: see its function group.
  5. SE80: create group ZFG_GR, then FM Z_GET_GR_TOTAL (L10 code). Test with F8.
  6. In your report: Pattern (Ctrl+F6) → Z_GET_GR_TOTAL → check sy-subrc.
  7. Copy the L13 macro into a test report, set a breakpoint, see that F5 can't go inside.

TESTQuick check

1. Which one is NOT called at runtime?

An Include is pasted in at compile time. It is never "called".

2. T-code to create a Function Module?

SE37 = Function Builder. SE24 = Class Builder. SE11 = DDIC.

3. FORM x USING p. You change p inside. The caller's variable?

USING without VALUE( ) is by reference. Use VALUE( ) to protect the caller.

4. FM has IMPORTING iv_ebeln. In CALL FUNCTION you write it under…

Mirror rule: caller EXPORTING = FM IMPORTING.

5. Which one is replaced by text at compile time?

Macro = Find & Replace before compile. You can't debug inside it.

6. Main program of function group ZFG_GR?

SAPL + group = main program. L + group + TOP = global data include.
Answer all 6 first.
⚡ Quick revise. Read top to bottom in 2 minutes. Forgot something? Switch to 📖 Learn.

🧩 Part 1 · Big idea

  • 🧠 One block, one name, call it many times.
  • 😫 Before: copy-paste → 4 fixes, one forgotten.
  • ✅ Why: reuse · easy to read · one fix · easy debug.

🧰 5 techniques · "I Sub Fun Met Mac"

WayKeywordT-codeScope
IncludeINCLUDESE38Pasted in, not called
SubroutinePERFORMSE38One program
Function ModuleCALL FUNCTIONSE37All programs + RFC
Method ⭐obj->m( )SE24All programs, modern
MacroDEFINESE38One program, no debug

☁️ ABAP Cloud = write classes, FORM not allowed, call only SAP-released APIs. FORM, Macro = obsolete.

📄 Include

  • Type I. Pasted at compile time. No input/output.
  • TOP data · O01 PBO · I01 PAI · F01 FORMs.
  • Photocopy 📄 vs FORM = phone call 📞.

🔁 Part 2 · FORM

PERFORM get_total USING p_ebeln CHANGING gv_total.
FORM get_total USING pv_ebeln TYPE ebeln
               CHANGING cv_total TYPE menge_d.
  • ➡️ USING = in · ⬅️ CHANGING = back · TABLES = obsolete.
  • 👫 Matched by position, not name.
  • 🔗 Default = reference: change inside = change outside.
  • 📄 VALUE( ) = copy, caller safe.
  • 🏠 DATA in FORM = local. STATICS keeps value.

🧰 Part 3 · Function Module (Q16)

CALL FUNCTION 'Z_GET_GR_TOTAL'
  EXPORTING iv_ebeln = p_ebeln   " = FM IMPORTING
  IMPORTING ev_total = gv_total
  EXCEPTIONS no_gr = 1 OTHERS = 2.
FORMFM
LivesOne programSE37, in a function group
CallPERFORMCALL FUNCTION 'NAME'
Params byPositionName, optional allowed
ErrorsNone built inEXCEPTIONS → sy-subrc
Test alone / RFCNoYes (F8 in SE37) / Yes

🪞 Caller EXPORTING = FM IMPORTING. 💡 Pattern (Ctrl+F6) writes the call. Wrong name → dump CALL_FUNCTION_NOT_FOUND.

🏠 Part 4 · Function Group (Q17)

  • Container (type F) for related FMs. Every FM belongs to one.
  • SAPLZFG_GR main · LZFG_GRTOP shared data · LZFG_GRUXX FM list (don't edit) · LZFG_GRU01 FM code.
  • First FM call loads the whole group. TOP data stays for the program run → clear old values.

⚡ Part 5 · Macro vs FORM (Q18)

  • DEFINE name … END-OF-DEFINITION. Placeholders &1–&9.
  • Macro = text replaced at compile time · FORM = block called at runtime.
  • Macro: no types, no local data, can't debug inside, define before use. Both obsolete → methods.
🪤 Traps: Include called? No. FORM in ABAP Cloud? No. USING without VALUE changes caller? Yes. Macro faster? No. Macro called at runtime? No.
🎤 30-SECOND ANSWER

"Modularization splits code into reusable named blocks: includes, subroutines, function modules (SE37), class methods (SE24) and macros. In my GR report I moved the MSEG 101 read into one FORM with USING for the PO and CHANGING for the total, then into a function module in function group ZFG_GR so several reports could share it and I could test it alone in SE37. Today I prefer class methods, as FORM and macros are obsolete and ABAP Cloud is built on classes."
