MODULE 04 · PDF Q20–25 · COMPLETE ✅

Reports & ALV

Ask the user what they want, read the data, show it nicely. T-codes: SE38, SE37, SE80, SA38.

😫 PROBLEMUsers need GR lists every day: by date, by PO, by material, and they want Excel.
💡 IDEAA report = screen to ask → SELECT to read → list to show. ALV gives sort, filter and Excel for free.
🔧 HOWEvents (INITIALIZATION → START-OF-SELECTION), WRITE lists, REUSE_ALV_GRID_DISPLAY, CL_SALV_TABLE.
➡️ NEXTModule 5: Performance (make this report fast).

LEARNLevel by level

Part 1 · Report types (Q20, Q21) Part 2 · Events (Q22) Part 3 · ALV (Q23, Q24) Part 4 · TOP-OF-PAGE (Q25)
PART 1 · REPORTS

🤔 What is a report?

A report is a program (type 1, made in SE38) that reads data and shows it. It does not change data.

Every report does 3 things:

1️⃣ Ask: selection screen→2️⃣ Read: SELECT→3️⃣ Show: list / ALV
  • 1️⃣ Ask: the user types a date range or a PO (PARAMETERS, SELECT-OPTIONS).
  • 2️⃣ Read: you SELECT from MKPF/MSEG with those values.
  • 3️⃣ Show: print with WRITE, or better, show in ALV.

🧱 The selection screen

DATA gv_budat TYPE mkpf-budat.
PARAMETERS     p_werks TYPE mseg-werks.   " one value
SELECT-OPTIONS s_budat FOR gv_budat.     " a range

🔑 BUDAT = posting date (Buchungsdatum). WERKS = plant (Werk). PARAMETERS = one box. SELECT-OPTIONS = "from–to" box with ranges, lists and exclusions. You use it with IN: WHERE budat IN @s_budat.

💡 A select-option is a small table with 4 columns: SIGN (I include / E exclude), OPTION (EQ, BT between …), LOW, HIGH.

▶️ Run a report: F8 in SE38, or SA38 (run any report by name), or its own T-code from SE93.

🧠 Remember: report = Ask → Read → Show. It reads, it doesn't change.

🗂️ Types of reports (Q20)

TypeOutputIn simple words
📄 ClassicalWRITE list, one pagePlain text list. User can only read and print
👆 InteractiveBasic list + up to 20 detail listsDouble-click a line to open details (drill-down)
📊 ALVGrid with toolbarSort, filter, totals, Excel, layouts. Standard today
🌳 Hierarchical ALVHeader + itemsOne PO line, its GR lines below
🔎 SAP QuerySQ01, no codeBuilt by clicking (functional / key users)
🕰️ LDB reportLogical databaseOld: SAP reads data for you via GET events. Obsolete

😫 Before ALV

LOOP AT gt_gr INTO DATA(ls_gr).
  WRITE: / ls_gr-mblnr, ls_gr-matnr, ls_gr-menge.
ENDLOOP.

🔑 WRITE: / = new line and print. Looks like a typed page. The user wants "sort by material" or "send to Excel"? You must code it yourself. 😩

🧠 Remember: classical = read only · interactive = drill-down · ALV = grid with toolbar.

👆 Classical vs Interactive (Q21)

Classical shows one list and stops. Interactive lets the user double-click a line and opens a secondary list with details.

START-OF-SELECTION.
  SELECT ebeln, lifnr FROM ekko INTO TABLE @DATA(lt_po) UP TO 20 ROWS.
  LOOP AT lt_po INTO DATA(ls_po).
    WRITE: / ls_po-ebeln, ls_po-lifnr.
    HIDE ls_po-ebeln.          " remember the PO behind this line
  ENDLOOP.

AT LINE-SELECTION.             " user double-clicked a line
  SELECT mblnr, menge FROM mseg
    WHERE ebeln = @ls_po-ebeln AND bwart = '101' INTO TABLE @DATA(lt_gr).
  LOOP AT lt_gr INTO DATA(ls_gr). WRITE: / ls_gr-mblnr, ls_gr-menge. ENDLOOP.

🚶 Step by step

  1. Basic list shows 20 POs: EKKO = PO header, LIFNR = vendor.
  2. HIDE saves the PO number secretly with each printed line.
  3. User double-clicks a PO → event AT LINE-SELECTION runs. SAP puts the hidden PO back into ls_po-ebeln.
  4. You read its 101 GRs and print them on a new list. Back arrow = return to the PO list.
📄 Classical👆 Interactive
Lists1Basic + up to 20 secondary
User actionRead, printDouble-click, buttons
Key eventsSTART-OF-SELECTION, TOP-OF-PAGE+ AT LINE-SELECTION, AT USER-COMMAND
Key toolsWRITE+ HIDE, sy-lsind, GET CURSOR

🔢 sy-lsind = list index: 0 = basic list, 1–20 = secondary lists. Above 20 → dump.

🎤 INTERVIEW (Q20, Q21)

"Report types are classical, interactive, ALV, plus SAP Query and old logical database reports. A classical report prints one WRITE list. An interactive report shows a basic list and up to 20 secondary lists: in my GR report the user double-clicked a PO, AT LINE-SELECTION read its 101 GRs from MSEG using the PO saved with HIDE, and sy-lsind told me the list level. Once the wrong PO opened because I forgot HIDE, so the variable kept the last loop value."

🧠 Remember: interactive = HIDE + AT LINE-SELECTION + sy-lsind (0–20).
PART 2 · EVENTS

🤔 What is an event? (Q22)

A report is not read top to bottom. It is cut into event blocks. SAP (the runtime) calls each block at the right moment, like a fixed timetable.

LOAD-OF-PROGRAM→INITIALIZATION→AT SELECTION-SCREEN OUTPUT→👤 user types, F8→AT SELECTION-SCREEN→START-OF-SELECTION→END-OF-SELECTION→📄 list shows→AT LINE-SELECTION
EventWhenUse it for
INITIALIZATIONBefore the screen shows, onceDefault values (date = last 30 days)
AT SELECTION-SCREEN OUTPUTBefore each screen displayHide / grey out fields
AT SELECTION-SCREENAfter user presses F8 / EnterCheck input. Error → screen stays open
START-OF-SELECTIONAfter checks passMain logic: SELECT, call ALV
END-OF-SELECTIONAfter START-OF-SELECTIONFinal output (old LDB style)
TOP-OF-PAGEAt the first WRITE of each new pagePage heading
END-OF-PAGEPage is fullFooter (needs LINE-COUNT)
AT LINE-SELECTIONUser double-clicks a list lineDetail list
AT USER-COMMANDUser clicks a toolbar buttonOwn buttons
🪤 Trap: code with no event keyword belongs to START-OF-SELECTION. It is the default event.
🧠 Remember: SAP calls the events in a fixed order. You only fill them.

🔧 A GR report with events

REPORT zgr_alv.
DATA gv_budat TYPE mkpf-budat.
SELECT-OPTIONS s_budat FOR gv_budat.

INITIALIZATION.
  APPEND VALUE #( sign = 'I' option = 'BT'
    low = sy-datum - 30 high = sy-datum ) TO s_budat.
AT SELECTION-SCREEN.
  IF s_budat[] IS INITIAL.
    MESSAGE 'Enter a posting date' TYPE 'E'.
  ENDIF.
START-OF-SELECTION.
  PERFORM get_gr.      " SELECT MKPF + MSEG
  PERFORM show_alv.    " display (Part 3)

🚶 Step by step

  1. INITIALIZATION: fills the date box with "today − 30 to today". sy-datum = today. BT = between.
  2. Screen shows. User changes dates and presses F8.
  3. AT SELECTION-SCREEN: box empty? TYPE 'E' error keeps the screen open so the user can fix it.
  4. START-OF-SELECTION: reads GRs and shows ALV.

📦 The GR read (FORM get_gr)

SELECT k~mblnr, k~budat, s~matnr, s~menge, s~ebeln
  FROM mkpf AS k INNER JOIN mseg AS s
    ON s~mblnr = k~mblnr AND s~mjahr = k~mjahr
  WHERE s~bwart = '101' AND k~budat IN @s_budat
  INTO TABLE @gt_gr.

🔑 k / s = short names (alias) for MKPF (header, has the date) and MSEG (items, has qty). Join key: MBLNR + MJAHR (doc no. + year). ~ = "field of this table". S/4: same code works, or read MATDOC directly.

🪤 Trap: s_budat[] with [] = the whole range table. s_budat alone = only its header line.
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q22)

"Report events are blocks the runtime calls in order: INITIALIZATION for defaults, AT SELECTION-SCREEN OUTPUT to change the screen, AT SELECTION-SCREEN to validate input, START-OF-SELECTION for the main logic, then list events like TOP-OF-PAGE and AT LINE-SELECTION. In my GR report INITIALIZATION set the last 30 days, AT SELECTION-SCREEN stopped empty dates with an E message, and START-OF-SELECTION read MKPF and MSEG and showed ALV. Once I put validation in START-OF-SELECTION and the E message ended the program, so I moved it to AT SELECTION-SCREEN where the screen stays open."

🧠 Remember: defaults in INITIALIZATION, checks in AT SELECTION-SCREEN, work in START-OF-SELECTION.

😂 Event tricks

  • 🥟 "I Ate Samosa, Then Lassi" = INITIALIZATION → AT SELECTION-SCREEN → START-OF-SELECTION → TOP-OF-PAGE → AT LINE-SELECTION.
  • 🚪 AT SELECTION-SCREEN = security guard: wrong ID? "Wapas jao, theek karo" → screen stays open.
  • 🏠 No event keyword? Code goes to START-OF-SELECTION, the default home.
  • 🔢 sy-lsind 20: "20 floors max in the drill-down building". Floor 21 = dump.
  • 🙈 HIDE = sticky note behind each line. Double-click reads the note.

🎮 Your turn (answer first, then tap)

PART 3 · ALV

🤔 What is ALV? (Q23)

ALV = ABAP List Viewer. You give it an internal table and a column list. SAP draws a grid with a full toolbar.

  • ❓ Why? Users want sort, filter, totals, Excel, saved layouts. With WRITE you code each one. With ALV they come free.
  • 🕰️ Before: WRITE lists with hand-made headings and no Excel.
  • 💡 Now: fill a table, call ALV, done.

🪜 3 steps for every ALV

  1. Data: fill internal table gt_gr (SELECT from Part 2).
  2. Columns: build the field catalog (which fields, which headings).
  3. Show: call the ALV function or class.

🧰 3 ways to make ALV

WayTypeUse when
REUSE_ALV_GRID_DISPLAYFunction module (old)Old projects, very common
CL_SALV_TABLEClass (modern, simple)New display-only reports ⭐
CL_GUI_ALV_GRIDClass (powerful)Editable cells, inside your own screen
🧠 Remember: ALV = data + field catalog + display. Toolbar is free.

🔧 REUSE_ALV_GRID_DISPLAY (old, still everywhere)

Remember the macro from Module 3 L13? It builds the field catalog here.

DATA: lt_fcat TYPE slis_t_fieldcat_alv,
      ls_fcat TYPE slis_fieldcat_alv.
add_fcat 'MBLNR' 'GR doc'.
add_fcat 'BUDAT' 'Posted on'.
add_fcat 'MENGE' 'Quantity'.

CALL FUNCTION 'REUSE_ALV_GRID_DISPLAY'
  EXPORTING i_callback_program     = sy-repid
            i_callback_top_of_page = 'TOP_OF_PAGE'
            it_fieldcat            = lt_fcat
  TABLES    t_outtab               = gt_gr.

🚶 What each line means

  • SLIS = type group with all old ALV types. slis_t_fieldcat_alv = table of columns, slis_fieldcat_alv = one column.
  • add_fcat = the Module 3 macro: fills fieldname + seltext_m and appends.
  • sy-repid = name of this program. ALV needs it to call your FORMs back.
  • 'TOP_OF_PAGE' = name of your FORM for the header (Part 4). Capital letters!
  • t_outtab = your data table gt_gr.
💡 Lazy option: REUSE_ALV_FIELDCATALOG_MERGE builds the catalog for you from a DDIC structure.
🪤 Trap: the field name in the catalog must match the table field exactly, in CAPITALS ('MBLNR'). Wrong name → empty column or dump.
🧠 Remember: REUSE ALV = field catalog + sy-repid + TABLES t_outtab.

🆚 LIST vs GRID (Q24)

📄 REUSE_ALV_LIST_DISPLAY📊 REUSE_ALV_GRID_DISPLAY
Looks likeClassical list (text page)Spreadsheet grid with cells
Built withList processing (WRITE inside)GUI control (CL_GUI_ALV_GRID inside)
ColumnsFixedDrag, resize, freeze
Header (TOP-OF-PAGE)Plain WRITE statementsREUSE_ALV_COMMENTARY_WRITE
Background jobPrints to spool directlyNo screen in background, so output goes out as a list
Best forPrinting, background runsUsers working on screen

🌳 Third brother: REUSE_ALV_HIERSEQ_LIST_DISPLAY = header lines with item lines below (PO → its GRs).

🎤 INTERVIEW (Q23, Q24)

"ALV is the ABAP List Viewer: I fill an internal table, build a field catalog and call the display, and users get sort, filter, totals and Excel for free. REUSE_ALV_LIST_DISPLAY gives a classical-style list that suits printing and background jobs, while REUSE_ALV_GRID_DISPLAY gives an interactive grid built on a GUI control. In my GR report I used the grid with I_CALLBACK_PROGRAM = sy-repid and a TOP_OF_PAGE form. One column stayed empty because I wrote the field name in lowercase in the catalog; after changing it to capitals it worked."

🧠 Remember: LIST = print-style, GRID = spreadsheet-style.

🚀 Modern: CL_SALV_TABLE

Same grid, much less code. No field catalog: SALV reads the column names and headings from the DDIC types of your table.

TRY.
    cl_salv_table=>factory(
      IMPORTING r_salv_table = DATA(lo_alv)
      CHANGING  t_table      = gt_gr ).
    lo_alv->get_functions( )->set_all( abap_true ).  " full toolbar
    lo_alv->get_columns( )->set_optimize( abap_true ). " fit widths
    lo_alv->display( ).
  CATCH cx_salv_msg INTO DATA(lx).
    MESSAGE lx->get_text( ) TYPE 'E'.
ENDTRY.

🚶 What each line means

  • =>factory( ) = static method that creates the ALV object (OOP, Module 6).
  • lo_alv = the ALV object. -> = call a method on it.
  • set_all( abap_true ) = switch on all toolbar buttons. abap_true = 'X'.
  • TRY … CATCH = class-style error handling instead of sy-subrc.
REUSE_ALVCL_SALV_TABLECL_GUI_ALV_GRID
StyleFMClassClass
Field catalogYou build itAutomaticYou build it
Editable cellsLimitedNoYes
Own screen neededNoNoYes (container)
⚠️ Honest: SALV is simpler, not faster. Speed comes from the SELECT (Module 5). See PDF fixes.
🧠 Remember: SALV = factory → display. No field catalog, no editing.
PART 4 · ALV HEADER

🤔 TOP-OF-PAGE in ALV (Q25)

The user wants a heading above the grid: report name, date, plant. In ALV you don't write it in the report. You give ALV the name of a FORM. ALV calls it back when it draws the top.

i_callback_top_of_page = 'TOP_OF_PAGE'→ALV draws grid→calls your FORM→header shows
FORM top_of_page.
  DATA lt_head TYPE slis_t_listheader.
  APPEND VALUE #( typ = 'H' info = 'GR Report · Mvt 101' ) TO lt_head.
  APPEND VALUE #( typ = 'S' key = 'Run date:'
                  info = |{ sy-datum DATE = USER }| ) TO lt_head.
  CALL FUNCTION 'REUSE_ALV_COMMENTARY_WRITE'
    EXPORTING it_list_commentary = lt_head.
ENDFORM.

🚶 What each line means

  • slis_t_listheader = table of header lines.
  • typ: H = big Header, S = Selection line (key + value), A = Action (small italic text).
  • |{ sy-datum DATE = USER }| = today's date in the user's format (like 17.10.2026).
  • REUSE_ALV_COMMENTARY_WRITE = prints those lines in the GRID header.
🪤 Traps: 1) pass the name in CAPITALS ('TOP_OF_PAGE'), or ALV can't find it and silently shows no header. 2) Forget i_callback_program = sy-repid → no callback at all. 3) In LIST display, TOP-OF-PAGE uses plain WRITE instead.

🚀 SALV way: build a CL_SALV_FORM_LAYOUT_GRID and pass it with lo_alv->set_top_of_list( ).

🎤 INTERVIEW (Q25)

"TOP-OF-PAGE in ALV gives the report header. With REUSE_ALV_GRID_DISPLAY I pass I_CALLBACK_PROGRAM = sy-repid and I_CALLBACK_TOP_OF_PAGE = 'TOP_OF_PAGE'; ALV calls that FORM, which fills a SLIS_T_LISTHEADER table with H, S and A lines and calls REUSE_ALV_COMMENTARY_WRITE. In my GR report it showed the title, plant and run date. At first the header didn't appear because I passed the form name in lowercase; in capitals it worked."

🧠 Remember: ALV header = callback FORM name in CAPS + COMMENTARY_WRITE with H / S / A lines.

😂 ALV tricks

  • 🪜 ALV recipe "D-C-D": Data → Catalog → Display. "Dal, Chawal, Dahi" 🍛.
  • 📄📊 LIST = Letter (print it). GRID = Grid like Excel (play with it).
  • 📞 Callback = "missed call do, ALV call back karega". Give the FORM name in CAPS, or ALV can't dial it.
  • 🏷️ H-S-A header lines: "Heading, Small details, Aside note".
  • 🏭 SALV factory = order a ready-made ALV from the factory. No catalog, no edit.

🎮 Your turn (answer first, then tap)

🎤 FULL MODULE ANSWER

"My GR report used a selection screen with SELECT-OPTIONS on posting date, INITIALIZATION for a 30-day default, and AT SELECTION-SCREEN to validate input. START-OF-SELECTION joined MKPF and MSEG for movement type 101 and showed the result in REUSE_ALV_GRID_DISPLAY with a field catalog and a TOP_OF_PAGE header through REUSE_ALV_COMMENTARY_WRITE. Earlier versions were classical WRITE lists with drill-down via AT LINE-SELECTION and HIDE. For new reports I use CL_SALV_TABLE, which needs no field catalog."

DOHands-on

⚠️ Not possible in BTP: no SE38, no selection screens, no WRITE lists, no REUSE_ALV, no SALV (they need SAP GUI). MKPF/MSEG are not in the trial either.

  1. Practice the logic: class with IF_OO_ADT_CLASSRUN, SELECT from your own Z table with a date range.
  2. out->write( lt_data ) → F9 shows a table in the console.
  3. The cloud "report" = CDS view + Fiori list report (Module 10).
  1. SE38: ZGR_ALV with the L5 code: INITIALIZATION default, AT SELECTION-SCREEN check.
  2. FORM get_gr with the MKPF + MSEG join. Run, check rows in the debugger.
  3. FORM show_alv with REUSE_ALV_GRID_DISPLAY + the macro field catalog + TOP_OF_PAGE.
  4. Copy the report, swap the display for CL_SALV_TABLE. Compare lines of code.
  5. Make a classical copy with HIDE + AT LINE-SELECTION: double-click a PO → its GRs.

TESTQuick check

1. Where do you put input checks so the screen stays open on error?

AT SELECTION-SCREEN runs after F8; an E message sends the user back to the screen.

2. Code without any event keyword belongs to…

START-OF-SELECTION is the default event.

3. Max number of secondary lists?

sy-lsind 0 = basic list, 1–20 = secondary lists.

4. Which ALV builds columns from DDIC types with no field catalog?

SALV factory reads the table's types. Simple, but no editing.

5. Header in REUSE_ALV_GRID_DISPLAY is printed by…

COMMENTARY_WRITE with H / S / A lines. WRITE is for LIST display.

6. Header callback FORM isn't called. Most likely?

Pass 'TOP_OF_PAGE' in capitals and i_callback_program = sy-repid.
Answer all 6 first.

📄 Part 1 · Report types (Q20, Q21)

  • Report = Ask (PARAMETERS / SELECT-OPTIONS) → Read (SELECT) → Show (WRITE / ALV). Type 1, SE38, run in SA38.
  • Types: classical · interactive · ALV · hierarchical ALV · SAP Query (SQ01) · LDB (old).
  • Interactive: HIDE + AT LINE-SELECTION. sy-lsind 0 = basic, 1–20 = secondary.
  • Select-option = range table: SIGN, OPTION, LOW, HIGH. Use IN @s_budat.

⏱️ Part 2 · Events (Q22) · "I Ate Samosa, Then Lassi"

EventJob
INITIALIZATIONDefaults, once
AT SELECTION-SCREEN OUTPUTChange screen fields
AT SELECTION-SCREENValidate. E message → screen stays
START-OF-SELECTIONMain logic (default event)
TOP-OF-PAGE / END-OF-PAGEPage header / footer
AT LINE-SELECTION / AT USER-COMMANDDouble-click / button

📦 GR read: MKPF ⋈ MSEG on MBLNR + MJAHR, bwart = '101', budat IN @s_budat.

📊 Part 3 · ALV (Q23, Q24)

  • ALV = Data → Catalog → Display. Toolbar (sort, filter, Excel) free.
  • REUSE_ALV_GRID_DISPLAY: i_callback_program = sy-repid, it_fieldcat, TABLES t_outtab. Types from SLIS. Field names in CAPS.
  • LIST = print-style, WRITE header, good for background. GRID = spreadsheet control, COMMENTARY_WRITE header.
  • CL_SALV_TABLE: factory( ) → display( ). No catalog, no edit, simpler (not faster). Editable → CL_GUI_ALV_GRID.

🏷️ Part 4 · TOP-OF-PAGE (Q25)

  • i_callback_top_of_page = 'TOP_OF_PAGE' (CAPS) → ALV calls your FORM.
  • FORM fills slis_t_listheader: H heading · S key + value · A small note → REUSE_ALV_COMMENTARY_WRITE.
  • SALV: set_top_of_list( ) with CL_SALV_FORM_LAYOUT_GRID.
🪤 Traps: No event keyword → START-OF-SELECTION. Validate in START-OF-SELECTION → E message ends the program. Lowercase FORM name → no header. Forgot HIDE → wrong line opens. SALV faster? No.

MODULE 05 · PDF Q26–31, Q70 · COMPLETE ✅

Performance

Find the slow part, then fix the database first, ABAP second. T-codes: ST05, SAT, ST12, SE11, SM50.

😫 PROBLEMThe GR ALV report takes 8 minutes. Users go for tea and the job times out.
💡 IDEAMeasure first, never guess. Most time is lost in the database: too many rows, columns or trips.
🔧 HOWST05 + SAT to find it. Fewer rows / columns / trips, JOIN or FAE, buffering, hashed tables.
➡️ NEXTModule 6: OOPs ABAP (SALV, classes).

LEARNLevel by level

Part 1 · Find the slow part (Q26, Q31) Part 2 · Database rules (Q27, Q30, Q28) Part 3 · Buffering (Q29) Part 4 · ABAP side (Q27) Part 5 · Scenario (Q70)
PART 1 · FIND IT

🤔 What is performance tuning?

Making a program finish faster and use less database, memory and CPU, while giving the same result.

  • ❓ Why? A slow report blocks a work process (a "worker" on the app server). Many slow reports = the whole system feels slow for everyone.
  • 🕰️ Before (the wrong way): guess. "Maybe this LOOP is slow", change random code, nothing improves.
  • 💡 Right way: measure → find the top 1–2 problems → fix → measure again.

⏱️ Where does the time go?

🗄️ Database (SELECT)+⚙️ ABAP (LOOP, READ)+🖥️ System (screen, memory)

In most slow reports the database part is the biggest. So check SQL first.

🧰 The tools

T-codeNameTells you
ST05Performance (SQL) traceEvery SELECT: time, rows, how often
SATRuntime analysis (old SE30)Time split DB vs ABAP, per statement / FORM / method
ST12Single transaction analysisSAT + ST05 together (if installed)
SM50 / SM66Work processesWhat a running job is doing right now
SQLM / SWLTSQL Monitor / worklistSlowest SQL in production (HANA projects)
🧠 Remember: measure first. DB first, ABAP second.

🔍 ST05: SQL trace (Q31)

  1. ST05 → Activate trace (for your user).
  2. Run your report in another session.
  3. Back in ST05 → Deactivate trace → Display trace.
  4. Read it: duration, number of records, table name, and the WHERE.
  5. Menu Trace list → Summarize / Identical selects: the same SELECT 1,000 times = a SELECT inside a LOOP.
📦
GR example: trace shows SELECT SINGLE … FROM ekko executed 4,800 times with the same few PO numbers. That's a SELECT in a LOOP. Fix: one JOIN or FAE (L5).

⏱️ SAT: runtime analysis

  1. SAT → enter the report name → Execute.
  2. Run with real selection values, then go back.
  3. Open the result: Hit list sorted by net time.
  4. See the split: % in database vs % in ABAP.
SAT showsMeaningNext step
DB 85 %SQL is the problemST05, fix SELECTs
ABAP 80 %LOOP / READ is the problemHashed tables, no nested loops (L7)

🔑 Net time = time inside that line only. Gross time = including everything it calls.

⚠️ PDF fix: SE30 is the old name. Say SAT today.
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q31)

"To analyze database performance I reproduce the run with real selection values, run SAT to see the split between database and ABAP time and the top statements, and use the ST05 SQL trace to see each SELECT's duration, rows and repeats; identical selects reveal SELECTs inside loops. ST12 combines both, SM50 shows what a running job is doing, and on HANA the SQL Monitor shows the heaviest statements in production. Then I fix the field list, WHERE clause, indexes and joins, and measure again with the same values."

🧠 Remember: ST05 = which SQL. SAT = DB vs ABAP split.
PART 2 · DATABASE

🚫 SELECT * vs needed fields (Q30)

" 😩 slow: all ~180 MSEG columns
SELECT * FROM mseg WHERE bwart = '101' INTO TABLE @DATA(lt_all).

" ✅ fast: only 4 columns the report shows
SELECT mblnr, matnr, menge, ebeln FROM mseg
  WHERE bwart = '101' INTO TABLE @DATA(lt_gr).

💥 What SELECT * costs

  • 🚚 Network: every row carries all columns from DB to app server. 1 lakh rows × 180 columns!
  • 🧠 Memory: the internal table becomes huge. Big jobs can dump (TSV_TNEW_PAGE_ALLOC_FAILED = no memory).
  • 📊 HANA: column store must rebuild every column into rows. Reading 4 columns is much cheaper.
  • 🔍 Index-only reads are lost: if all fields are in an index, the DB could skip the table. Not with *.
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q30)

"SELECT * moves every column from the database to the application server, so it costs network time, memory and, on HANA, extra work because the column store must rebuild full rows. In my GR report SELECT * on MSEG with 1 lakh rows was replaced by the 4 fields the ALV showed, and the read time dropped a lot in ST05. I keep SELECT * only for small tables when I really need all fields."

🧠 Remember: select only what you show.

🏅 5 golden rules for SQL (Q27)

#RuleHow
1📉 Fewer rowsGood WHERE (date, plant, mvt type). No filtering later in ABAP
2📏 Fewer columnsNo SELECT * (L3)
3🔁 Fewer tripsNo SELECT in LOOP. Use JOIN, FAE, one array SELECT
4🔍 Use an indexWHERE on key or index fields, in index order
5🧮 Let the DB calculateSUM, COUNT, GROUP BY in SQL (HANA module)

😫 Rule 3 broken: SELECT in LOOP

LOOP AT lt_gr INTO DATA(ls_gr).
  SELECT SINGLE lifnr FROM ekko
    WHERE ebeln = @ls_gr-ebeln INTO @DATA(lv_lifnr).
ENDLOOP.   " 5,000 GR lines = 5,000 DB trips 😱

Each trip has a fixed cost (send, parse, return). 5,000 small trips are much slower than 1 big trip.

🔍 Rule 4: indexes

  • 📖 An index = like a book index: the DB jumps to the rows instead of reading the whole table.
  • 🔑 Primary index = the table key (MSEG: MANDT, MBLNR, MJAHR, ZEILE). Always there.
  • ➕ Secondary index = extra index made in SE11 → Indexes. Ask before creating one on a big table: it slows every insert.
  • 🪤 WHERE matnr LIKE '%ABC' or <> often can't use an index.
🧠 Remember: fewer rows, fewer columns, fewer trips, use index, DB calculates.

🆚 FOR ALL ENTRIES vs INNER JOIN (Q28)

Both fix "SELECT in LOOP": they read data for many POs in one go.

🔗 INNER JOIN: one SELECT, DB links the tables

SELECT k~ebeln, k~lifnr, s~mblnr, s~menge
  FROM ekko AS k INNER JOIN mseg AS s ON s~ebeln = k~ebeln
  WHERE k~bedat IN @s_bedat AND s~bwart = '101'
  INTO TABLE @DATA(lt_join).

📋 FOR ALL ENTRIES: 2nd SELECT uses the 1st table

SELECT ebeln, lifnr FROM ekko WHERE bedat IN @s_bedat
  INTO TABLE @DATA(lt_po).
IF lt_po IS NOT INITIAL.          " ⚠️ never skip this
  SELECT mblnr, mjahr, zeile, ebeln, menge FROM mseg
    FOR ALL ENTRIES IN @lt_po
    WHERE ebeln = @lt_po-ebeln AND bwart = '101'
    INTO TABLE @DATA(lt_gr).
ENDIF.

🔑 BEDAT = PO date (Bestelldatum). ZEILE = line no. inside the material doc. MBLNR + MJAHR + ZEILE = full key of MSEG.

🪤 3 FAE traps

  1. Empty driver table → the WHERE on it is ignored → SAP reads ALL MSEG rows for 101! Always check IS NOT INITIAL.
  2. Duplicates removed: FAE deletes identical result rows. Select the full key (MBLNR, MJAHR, ZEILE) or two GR lines with the same qty become one.
  3. Duplicates in driver: sort + DELETE ADJACENT DUPLICATES on lt_po first, so fewer packages go to the DB.
🔗 INNER JOIN📋 FOR ALL ENTRIES
DB trips12+ (SAP splits into packages)
Who links tablesDatabaseABAP sends a list of keys
Empty table riskNoYes: reads everything
DuplicatesKeptRemoved
Aggregates (SUM)YesLimited
Best whenTables in same DB, good keys. Preferred on HANADriver data from a file / BAPI / earlier logic; buffered or pool tables
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q28)

"An INNER JOIN reads related tables in one SELECT and the database links them, so it's usually fastest, especially on HANA. FOR ALL ENTRIES runs a second SELECT for the keys in an internal table; I must check the table is not empty, otherwise the condition is ignored and all rows are read, and I select the full key because FAE removes duplicate rows. In my GR report an empty PO table with FAE once read every 101 movement in MSEG; adding the IS NOT INITIAL check fixed it, and later I replaced it with a join of EKKO and MSEG."

🧠 Remember: JOIN = 1 trip, DB links. FAE = check not empty + full key.
PART 3 · BUFFERING

🤔 What is table buffering? (Q29)

SAP keeps a copy of a table in the app server memory. A SELECT on it is answered from memory, without going to the database. Up to ~100× faster.

  • ❓ Why? Small tables like plants (T001W) are read thousands of times a day. No need to ask the DB each time.
  • ⚙️ Where? SE11 → table → Technical settings → Buffering.
TypeWhat is loadedGood for
📦 FullWhole table on first readVery small tables (a few hundred rows)
🔑 GenericAll rows with the same first key fields (e.g. one client + one plant)Medium tables read by key prefix
☝️ Single recordOnly the rows you read with SELECT SINGLEBig tables, read one row at a time
" T001W (plants) is usually buffered → answered from memory
SELECT SINGLE name1 FROM t001w
  WHERE werks = @p_werks INTO @DATA(lv_plant_name).

✅ Buffer when · ❌ don't buffer when

✅ Good❌ Bad
SmallBig (MSEG, BSEG)
Read oftenChanged often (every GR changes MSEG)
Rarely changed (config, plants, mvt types T156)Must always be 100 % current
🪤 These SELECTs skip the buffer: BYPASSING BUFFER, JOINs, aggregates (SUM, COUNT), ORDER BY on non-key fields, SELECT … FOR UPDATE, Native SQL. Also: other app servers see changes only after a short sync delay.

📈 Check: ST10 = table call statistics (which tables are read most, and from buffer or DB).

🎤 INTERVIEW (Q29)

"Table buffering keeps a copy of a table in the application server's memory, so SELECTs are answered without a database call. It's set in SE11 technical settings as full, generic or single-record buffering, and fits small, read-often, rarely changed tables like plants or movement types, not MSEG. JOINs, aggregates and BYPASSING BUFFER skip it. For our custom config table ZGR_SETTINGS I switched on full buffering and the reads disappeared from the ST05 trace."

🧠 Remember: buffer = small + read often + rarely changed. Full / Generic / Single.
PART 4 · ABAP SIDE

📒 Fast internal tables

Once the DB part is good, SAT may still show 80 % in ABAP. Usually it's searching big internal tables.

" ✅ hashed lookup: same speed for 10 or 10 lakh rows
DATA lt_po TYPE HASHED TABLE OF ekko WITH UNIQUE KEY ebeln.
" ✅ sorted table: LOOP WHERE uses the key, no full scan
DATA lt_gr TYPE SORTED TABLE OF ty_gr WITH NON-UNIQUE KEY ebeln.

LOOP AT lt_po ASSIGNING FIELD-SYMBOL(<po>).
  LOOP AT lt_gr ASSIGNING FIELD-SYMBOL(<gr>) WHERE ebeln = <po>-ebeln.
    " only this PO's GRs, found by key
  ENDLOOP.
ENDLOOP.
😩 Slow✅ Fast
READ TABLE on big standard tableHashed / sorted table, or BINARY SEARCH after SORT
Nested LOOP on two standard tablesInner table SORTED, LOOP … WHERE on its key
LOOP INTO work area + MODIFYASSIGNING FIELD-SYMBOL (no copy)
DELETE rows one by one in a loopOne DELETE … WHERE
Collect totals in ABAPSUM … GROUP BY in SQL
🪤 Trap: READ TABLE … BINARY SEARCH on an unsorted table gives wrong results silently. SORT by the same key first.

🔗 Hashed vs sorted vs standard details: Basics tab, L2.

🧠 Remember: hashed for READ, sorted for LOOP WHERE, field symbols for changes.

🚀 On HANA: push the work down

On HANA the DB is the fastest floor (HANA tab). So move loops and totals into SQL.

" 😩 old: read every line, add in ABAP
LOOP AT lt_gr INTO DATA(ls). COLLECT ls INTO lt_sum. ENDLOOP.
" ✅ new: DB adds, returns 300 rows not 5 lakh
SELECT matnr, SUM( menge ) AS qty FROM mseg
  WHERE bwart = '101' GROUP BY matnr INTO TABLE @DATA(lt_sum).
  • 🪜 Order: ABAP SQL (SUM, CASE, JOIN) → CDS view (reusable) → AMDP (very complex logic).
  • 🔀 Very big jobs: split the work and run in parallel (aRFC / background tasks, Module 12).
  • 🧭 SCI / ATC = code checks. They warn about SELECT in LOOP, SELECT * and nested loops before you transport.
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q26, Q27)

"To optimize a program I first measure with SAT and ST05, then fix the database part: select only needed fields with a good WHERE, avoid SELECT in loops using joins or FOR ALL ENTRIES, use indexes and buffering, and push totals into SQL on HANA. Then I fix the ABAP part with hashed or sorted tables and field symbols. I also run ATC to catch these patterns early. In my GR report these steps took the runtime from minutes to seconds."

🧠 Remember: on HANA, the DB calculates. ATC catches bad patterns.
PART 5 · SCENARIO

🎭 "Your ALV report takes too long. What do you do?" (Q70)

  1. 🧪 Reproduce: same selection values as the user (dates, plant). Note the time.
  2. ⏱️ SAT: hit list, DB vs ABAP split. Find the top 1–2 statements.
  3. 🔍 ST05: which SELECTs, how many times, how many rows. Look for identical selects.
  4. 🗄️ Fix SQL: needed fields only, WHERE on key / index fields, JOIN or FAE instead of SELECT in LOOP, totals in SQL.
  5. 📒 Fix ABAP: hashed / sorted tables, LOOP WHERE, field symbols, no nested standard loops.
  6. 🎯 Limit data: make key selection fields mandatory, default a short date range, run huge selections as a background job.
  7. 📏 Measure again with the same values. Compare before / after. Same output?
📦
Example story: GR ALV for one plant, 3 months. SAT: 88 % DB. ST05: SELECT * on MSEG + 4,800 SELECT SINGLE on EKKO in the loop. Fix: 6 fields, one EKKO + MSEG join, hashed table for vendor names. Result: 8 min → 40 s.
⚠️ Don't say: "I'll switch to SALV, it's faster." The grid is not the slow part; the data reading is.
🎤 INTERVIEW (Q70)

"I'd first reproduce it with the user's selection and run SAT to see whether time goes to the database or ABAP, then ST05 to find the expensive SELECTs. In one GR ALV report 88 percent was database: SELECT * on MSEG and thousands of SELECT SINGLE on EKKO inside the loop. I selected only the needed fields, replaced the loop selects with one join of EKKO and MSEG, used a hashed table for lookups and made plant mandatory. Runtime went from about 8 minutes to 40 seconds, and I compared the output before and after."

🧠 Remember: Reproduce → SAT → ST05 → fix SQL → fix ABAP → limit data → measure again.

😂 Performance tricks

  • 🩺 Doctor rule: "Pehle test, phir dawai". Measure (SAT, ST05) before you change code.
  • 🕵️ ST05 = CCTV of the database: every SELECT is recorded. SAT = stopwatch for each line.
  • 🍕 SELECT * = ordering the whole menu to eat one pizza.
  • 🛺 SELECT in LOOP = 5,000 auto rides for 5,000 parcels. JOIN = one truck 🚚.
  • 🫙 FAE empty table = "khaali dabba, poora godown aa gaya": empty list → ALL rows come.
  • 🧊 Buffer = fridge at home: keep small things you use daily (plants, config). Not the whole market (MSEG).

🎮 Your turn (answer first, then tap)

DOHands-on

⚠️ Not in BTP: no ST05 / SAT screens (SAP GUI tools), no MSEG. The SQL rules are the same.

  1. Class with IF_OO_ADT_CLASSRUN: fill a Z table, then read it with SELECT * and with 3 fields.
  2. Right-click the class → Profile As → ABAP Application (Console).
  3. Open the profiler result: compare DB time of both SELECTs.
  4. Try a hashed vs standard READ in a loop of 1 lakh rows and compare.
  1. Take your GR ALV (Module 4). SAT → run it → note DB % and top lines.
  2. ST05 → activate → run → display. Look for identical selects.
  3. Change SELECT * to needed fields; replace a SELECT in LOOP with a JOIN. Measure again.
  4. Debugger: run FAE with an empty driver table on a test system and see how many rows come back (use a small table!).
  5. SE11 → T001W → Technical settings: see its buffering type.

TESTQuick check

1. Which T-code shows every SQL statement with time and rows?

ST05 = SQL trace. SAT = runtime analysis (DB vs ABAP split).

2. FOR ALL ENTRIES with an empty driver table…

The FAE condition is ignored. Always check IS NOT INITIAL.

3. Why select the full key with FAE?

Two GR lines with identical selected values would become one.

4. Which table should NOT be buffered?

MSEG is big and changes with every goods movement.

5. Which SELECT skips the table buffer?

Aggregates, joins, BYPASSING BUFFER, FOR UPDATE go to the DB.

6. Slow ALV. First step?

Measure first. The grid is rarely the slow part.
Answer all 6 first.
⚡ Quick revise. Read top to bottom in 2 minutes. Forgot something? Switch to 📖 Learn.

🔍 Part 1 · Find it (Q26, Q31)

  • Measure first. DB first, ABAP second.
  • ST05 SQL trace: activate → run → deactivate → display. Identical selects = SELECT in LOOP.
  • SAT (old SE30): hit list, net time, DB vs ABAP %. ST12 = both. SM50 = live work processes. SQLM = prod SQL monitor.

🗄️ Part 2 · SQL rules (Q27, Q30, Q28)

  • 5 rules: fewer rows · fewer columns · fewer trips · use index · DB calculates.
  • SELECT * = more network, memory, HANA row rebuild, no index-only read.
  • JOIN = 1 trip, DB links, keeps duplicates, preferred on HANA.
  • FAE = check IS NOT INITIAL (else ALL rows) · select full key (duplicates removed) · dedupe driver.

🧊 Part 3 · Buffering (Q29)

  • Copy of table in app server memory. SE11 → Technical settings.
  • Full (tiny tables) · Generic (key prefix) · Single record (SELECT SINGLE).
  • Buffer: small, read often, rarely changed (T001W, T156, config). Not MSEG.
  • Skipped by: BYPASSING BUFFER, JOIN, SUM/COUNT, ORDER BY non-key, FOR UPDATE. Stats: ST10.

📒 Part 4 · ABAP side

  • Hashed for READ · sorted for LOOP WHERE · field symbols for changes · one DELETE WHERE.
  • BINARY SEARCH only after SORT by the same key.
  • HANA: SUM / GROUP BY in SQL → CDS → AMDP. ATC / SCI catch bad patterns.

🎭 Part 5 · Slow ALV (Q70)

Reproduce → SAT → ST05 → fix SQL → fix ABAP → limit data / background → measure again. Story: 88 % DB, SELECT * + loop SELECTs → fields + JOIN + hashed → 8 min → 40 s.

🪤 Traps: SE30 → say SAT. SALV faster? No. FAE empty table → all rows. Buffer MSEG? No. BINARY SEARCH unsorted → wrong result.
🎤 30-SECOND ANSWER

"I measure first with SAT and ST05. Then I fix the database part: only needed fields, good WHERE on indexed fields, joins or FOR ALL ENTRIES with a not-empty check instead of selects in loops, buffering for small config tables, and totals in SQL on HANA. Then ABAP: hashed or sorted tables and field symbols. That took my GR ALV from 8 minutes to 40 seconds."

OOPs ABAP

Classes and objects, step by step, with GR examples. T-codes: SE24 (global class), SE38 (local class), ADT.

😫 PROBLEMOld reports: global data + many FORMs. Any FORM can change any variable. Big programs break easily.
💡 IDEAPack data and the code that uses it into one box (a class). Others use only its public buttons.
🔧 HOWCLASS … DEFINITION / IMPLEMENTATION, NEW, methods, PUBLIC / PRIVATE, inheritance, interfaces.
➡️ NEXTModule 7: Enhancements & BADI (BADIs are interfaces!).
