HANDS-ON LAB · BTP TRIAL (ADT) · YOU WRITE THE CODE

CDS Lab: views, annotations, debugging

10 small steps. Read the task, write the code yourself in ADT, then tap 💡 Hint or ✅ Solution only if stuck.

😫 PROBLEMReading about CDS is easy to forget. Writing it once with your own hands makes it stick.
💡 IDEABuild one small GR model step by step: table → data → view → association → parameters → UI → Fiori.
🔧 HOWEclipse ADT on your BTP trial. F8 to preview. Fix every error yourself with the 🐞 tables.
➡️ NEXTAfter each step, tell me in chat "Step N done" + paste your code or error. I'll check it.
🧠 Step order hook: "Tea First, Very Cold, Add Pepper, Use Sugar, Drink" ☕ = Table → Fill → View → Calculate → Association → Parameter → UI → Service → Debug.

LEARNStep by step

Part 1 · Base (T, F) Part 2 · Views (V, C, A, P) Part 3 · Annotations & Fiori (U, S) Part 4 · Debug (D) and remember
STEP 0 · SETUP

🛠️ Before you start

  1. Open Eclipse ADT → your BTP trial project (ABAP Cloud). If F9 on any console class works, you're ready.
  2. Package: use ZLOCAL (local, no transport), or create your own sub-package, e.g. ZGR_LAB_KV.
  3. ⚠️ Names must be unique: the trial system is shared by many users. Add your own suffix to every object. This lab uses _KV; use your initials if _KV is taken.
  4. Keep this tab open on your phone and ADT on the laptop.
Key in ADTDoes
Ctrl+NNew object (table, data definition, class…)
Ctrl+F3Activate
F8Data Preview of a table / CDS view
F9Run a console class
F2Element info (hover help on any name)
Ctrl+SpaceCode completion (use it a lot!)
🧠 Remember: Ctrl+N new · Ctrl+F3 activate · F8 preview · F9 run · F2 info · Ctrl+Space complete.
STEP 1 · T = TABLE

🎯 Goal: two small tables

MSEG / EKKO don't exist in the trial, so we make mini copies: GR items and PO headers.

✍️ Your task

  1. Ctrl+N → Database Table → name ZGR_ITEM_KV, description "GR items (lab)".
  2. Fields: client (key), mblnr GR doc no. (key, char 10), mjahr year (key, numc 4), zeile item (key, numc 4), matnr material (char 40), werks plant (char 4), ebeln PO no. (char 10), bwart movement type (char 3), menge quantity (quan 13,3), meins unit (unit 3).
  3. Tell the system that menge belongs to meins (a @Semantics annotation above menge).
  4. Second table ZPO_HEAD_KV: client, ebeln (keys), lifnr vendor (char 10), bedat PO date (abap.dats).
  5. Ctrl+F3 activate both.
💡 Hint (tap)
ADT gives a template with 4 @AbapCatalog lines. Keep them. Built-in types look like abap.char(10), abap.numc(4), abap.quan(13,3), abap.unit(3), abap.clnt, abap.dats. Key fields need not null.
✅ Solution (tap only after trying) 
@EndUserText.label : 'GR items (lab)'
@AbapCatalog.enhancement.category : #NOT_EXTENSIBLE
@AbapCatalog.tableCategory : #TRANSPARENT
@AbapCatalog.deliveryClass : #A
@AbapCatalog.dataMaintenance : #RESTRICTED
define table zgr_item_kv {
  key client : abap.clnt not null;
  key mblnr  : abap.char(10) not null;
  key mjahr  : abap.numc(4) not null;
  key zeile  : abap.numc(4) not null;
  matnr      : abap.char(40);
  werks      : abap.char(4);
  ebeln      : abap.char(10);
  bwart      : abap.char(3);
  @Semantics.quantity.unitOfMeasure : 'zgr_item_kv.meins'
  menge      : abap.quan(13,3);
  meins      : abap.unit(3);
}
define table zpo_head_kv {        // same 5 @ lines on top
  key client : abap.clnt not null;
  key ebeln  : abap.char(10) not null;
  lifnr      : abap.char(10);
  bedat      : abap.dats;
}

🔑 #A = delivery class A (business data, DDIC tab L4). client first = client-dependent. The @Semantics…unitOfMeasure line = "menge is measured in meins".

🔍 You should see

Both tables active (no red ✖). F8 on a table shows "no data" for now.

🐞 If it fails

Error saysFix
Name already existsShared trial: change the suffix (_KV2, your initials)
Reference field / unit missing for MENGEAdd the @Semantics.quantity.unitOfMeasure line with the right table.field
Key field must not be nullableAdd not null to every key field
🧠 Remember: table = define table + 5 @ lines + keys first with not null + quantity needs its unit.
STEP 2 · F = FILL

🎯 Goal: put test data in

✍️ Your task

  1. Ctrl+N → ABAP Class ZCL_GR_FILL_KV, add interface if_oo_adt_classrun.
  2. In method if_oo_adt_classrun~main: first delete all rows of both tables (so you can run it again).
  3. Insert 3 PO headers and 6 GR items: 4 items with bwart = '101', 1 with '102' (reversal), 1 with '201'. Use 2 plants (1000, 2000) and unit ST (pieces).
  4. Write "Done" with out->write( ). F9.
💡 Hint (tap)
DELETE FROM zgr_item_kv. deletes all rows of your client. Insert many rows with INSERT zgr_item_kv FROM TABLE @( VALUE #( ( … ) ( … ) ) ). Each ( ) is one row: ( mblnr = '5000000001' mjahr = '2026' zeile = '0001' … ). You don't fill client: SAP does.
✅ Solution 
METHOD if_oo_adt_classrun~main.
  DELETE FROM zgr_item_kv.  DELETE FROM zpo_head_kv.
  INSERT zpo_head_kv FROM TABLE @( VALUE #(
    ( ebeln = '4500000001' lifnr = 'V100' bedat = '20261001' )
    ( ebeln = '4500000002' lifnr = 'V200' bedat = '20261005' )
    ( ebeln = '4500000003' lifnr = 'V100' bedat = '20261010' ) ) ).
  INSERT zgr_item_kv FROM TABLE @( VALUE #( mjahr = '2026' meins = 'ST'
    ( mblnr = '5000000001' zeile = '0001' matnr = 'MAT-A' werks = '1000' ebeln = '4500000001' bwart = '101' menge = 50 )
    ( mblnr = '5000000001' zeile = '0002' matnr = 'MAT-B' werks = '1000' ebeln = '4500000001' bwart = '101' menge = 120 )
    ( mblnr = '5000000002' zeile = '0001' matnr = 'MAT-A' werks = '2000' ebeln = '4500000002' bwart = '101' menge = 300 )
    ( mblnr = '5000000003' zeile = '0001' matnr = 'MAT-C' werks = '2000' ebeln = '4500000003' bwart = '101' menge = 10 )
    ( mblnr = '5000000004' zeile = '0001' matnr = 'MAT-B' werks = '1000' ebeln = '4500000001' bwart = '102' menge = 20 )
    ( mblnr = '5000000005' zeile = '0001' matnr = 'MAT-C' werks = '1000' ebeln = ''           bwart = '201' menge = 5 ) ) ).
  out->write( |Done: { sy-dbcnt } GR rows| ).
ENDMETHOD.

🔑 Values written before the first ( (mjahr, meins) are used for every row. sy-dbcnt = number of rows just inserted (6).

🔍 You should see

Console: "Done: 6 GR rows". F8 on ZGR_ITEM_KV shows 6 rows.

🐞 If it fails

ProblemFix
Duplicate key dump on second runThe DELETE FROM lines must come first
Unit 'ST' not acceptedTry 'PC' or 'EA'; trial unit lists differ
F9 does nothingDid you add if_oo_adt_classrun to the PUBLIC SECTION and activate?
🧠 Remember: DELETE first → INSERT FROM TABLE @( VALUE #( common fields ( row ) ( row ) ) ) → F9 → F8.
STEP 3 · V = VIEW

🎯 Goal: first CDS view entity (GR 101 only)

✍️ Your task

  1. Ctrl+N → Data Definition → ZI_GR_ITEM_KV, "GR items 101". Template: Define View Entity.
  2. Source table zgr_item_kv. Take the 4 keys and the other fields, give each a CamelCase alias (MaterialDocument, DocumentYear, DocumentItem, Material, Plant, PurchaseOrder, MovementType, Quantity, Unit).
  3. Only rows with movement type 101.
  4. Keep the 2 annotations from the template on top. Activate → F8.
💡 Hint (tap)
Shape: define view entity X as select from table { key field as Alias, … } where …. Commas between fields, no comma after the last. The where uses the table field name (bwart). Quantity needs @Semantics.quantity.unitOfMeasure: 'Unit' (the alias of the unit).
✅ Solution 
@AccessControl.authorizationCheck: #NOT_REQUIRED
@EndUserText.label: 'GR items 101'
define view entity ZI_GR_ITEM_KV
  as select from zgr_item_kv
{
  key mblnr as MaterialDocument,
  key mjahr as DocumentYear,
  key zeile as DocumentItem,
      matnr as Material,
      werks as Plant,
      ebeln as PurchaseOrder,
      bwart as MovementType,
      @Semantics.quantity.unitOfMeasure: 'Unit'
      menge as Quantity,
      meins as Unit
}
where bwart = '101'

🔍 You should see

F8: 4 rows (the 102 and 201 rows are filtered out). Column headers = your aliases.

🐞 If it fails

Error saysFix
Key elements must be at the beginningPut all key fields first
Reference to unit missing / QuantityAdd the @Semantics.quantity.unitOfMeasure: 'Unit' line and keep Unit in the list
Unexpected word / commaNo comma after the last field; commas everywhere else
F8 shows 6 rowsThe where is missing or not activated (Ctrl+F3)
🧠 Remember: 2 @ on top · define view entity · as select from · { key … as Alias, … } · where (table names).
STEP 4 · C = CALCULATE

🎯 Goal: a calculated field with CASE

CDS can calculate new columns in the database. Here: a readable text for the movement type.

✍️ Your task

  1. Make a copy view ZI_GR_MOVE_KV on zgr_item_kv without the where (all 6 rows).
  2. Add field MovementText: 101 → 'Goods receipt', 102 → 'GR reversal', anything else → 'Other'.
  3. Bonus: field DocItemKey = mblnr and zeile joined with concat( ).
💡 Hint (tap)
case bwart when '101' then '…' when '102' then '…' else '…' end as MovementText. Concat: concat( mblnr, zeile ) as DocItemKey.
✅ Solution (only the new lines) 
      case bwart
        when '101' then 'Goods receipt'
        when '102' then 'GR reversal'
        else 'Other'
      end as MovementText,
      concat( mblnr, zeile ) as DocItemKey,

🔍 You should see

F8: 6 rows, MovementText filled; the 201 row says "Other".

🐞 If it fails

ProblemFix
Missing alias for expressionEvery CASE / function needs as Name
Texts cut shortResult length comes from the longest literal; that's normal
🧠 Remember: case … when … then … else … end as Name. Every calculation needs an alias.
STEP 5 · A = ASSOCIATION

🎯 Goal: link GR items to their PO header

✍️ Your task

  1. New view ZI_PO_HEAD_KV on zpo_head_kv: key ebeln as PurchaseOrder, lifnr as Vendor, bedat as OrderDate.
  2. In ZI_GR_ITEM_KV add an association named _PO to ZI_PO_HEAD_KV, cardinality 0 or 1, linked on the PO number.
  3. Expose _PO at the end of the field list. Activate.
  4. F8 on ZI_GR_ITEM_KV → click a row → follow the association _PO (the arrow ▸ next to the result).
💡 Hint (tap)
The association line goes after as select from zgr_item_kv and before {: association [0..1] to ZI_PO_HEAD_KV as _PO on $projection.PurchaseOrder = _PO.PurchaseOrder. Then add _PO as the last element (comma before it!).
✅ Solution 
define view entity ZI_GR_ITEM_KV
  as select from zgr_item_kv
  association [0..1] to ZI_PO_HEAD_KV as _PO
    on $projection.PurchaseOrder = _PO.PurchaseOrder
{
  key mblnr as MaterialDocument,
  ...
      meins as Unit,
      _PO
}
where bwart = '101'

🔍 You should see

Data preview of a GR row → follow _PO → its PO header with Vendor V100 / V200.

🐞 If it fails

Error saysFix
$projection.PurchaseOrder unknownThe field must be in the list with exactly that alias
ZI_PO_HEAD_KV not foundActivate the PO view first
Association not visible to usersYou forgot to expose _PO in the field list
🧠 Remember: association [0..1] to TARGET as _Name on $projection.X = _Name.X, then expose _Name. A lazy join: runs only when used.
STEP 6 · P = PARAMETER

🎯 Goal: a view that asks for the plant

✍️ Your task

  1. New view ZI_GR_BYPLANT_KV with a parameter p_werks (char 4).
  2. Select from ZI_GR_ITEM_KV (a view on a view!) and keep only rows of that plant.
  3. F8 → ADT asks for p_werks → enter 1000.
💡 Hint (tap)
with parameters p_werks : abap.char(4) comes right after the view name. Use it as $parameters.p_werks. Here the source is a view, so the where uses the alias: Plant.
✅ Solution 
@AccessControl.authorizationCheck: #NOT_REQUIRED
@EndUserText.label: 'GR items by plant'
define view entity ZI_GR_BYPLANT_KV
  with parameters p_werks : abap.char(4)
  as select from ZI_GR_ITEM_KV
{
  key MaterialDocument,
  key DocumentYear,
  key DocumentItem,
      Material,
      Plant,
      @Semantics.quantity.unitOfMeasure: 'Unit'
      Quantity,
      Unit
}
where Plant = $parameters.p_werks

🔍 You should see

Plant 1000 → 2 rows (MAT-A 50, MAT-B 120). Plant 2000 → 2 rows.

📞 In ABAP: SELECT * FROM zi_gr_byplant_kv( p_werks = '1000' ) INTO TABLE @DATA(lt).

🧠 Remember: with parameters p : type → $parameters.p → call with view( p = value ). Source = view → use aliases.
STEP 7 · USE IN ABAP

🎯 Goal: read your CDS view from a class, with a path

✍️ Your task

  1. Class ZCL_GR_READ_KV with if_oo_adt_classrun.
  2. SELECT from zi_gr_item_kv: material, quantity, and the vendor through the association.
  3. Total quantity per vendor (SUM + GROUP BY).
  4. out->write( ) the result → F9.
💡 Hint (tap)
Path in ABAP SQL: \_po-vendor (backslash, underscore name, dash, field). For totals: SUM( quantity ) AS total and GROUP BY \_po-vendor.
✅ Solution 
METHOD if_oo_adt_classrun~main.
  SELECT FROM zi_gr_item_kv
    FIELDS \_po-vendor AS vendor, SUM( quantity ) AS total
    GROUP BY \_po-vendor
    INTO TABLE @DATA(lt_vendor).
  out->write( lt_vendor ).
ENDMETHOD.

🔍 You should see

Only 101 rows count. PO 4500000001 and 4500000003 belong to V100, PO 4500000002 to V200. So V100 = 180 (50 + 120 + 10) and V200 = 300. Work it out on paper first, then check the console.

🐞 If it fails

ProblemFix
Association _PO unknownExpose _PO in the view (Step 5)
Units mixed warning on SUMOK in the lab (all ST). Real life: group by Unit too
🧠 Remember: CDS path in ABAP = \_assoc-field. SUM / GROUP BY run in the DB (code pushdown).
STEP 8 · U = UI ANNOTATIONS

🎯 Goal: describe a Fiori list with annotations

Annotations are notes for the system. @UI notes tell Fiori: "show this column at position 10, put this field in the filter bar".

✍️ Your task

  1. Consumption view ZC_GR_ITEM_KV: as select from ZI_GR_ITEM_KV, all fields + _PO. On top: @Metadata.allowExtensions: true.
  2. Create a Metadata Extension (Ctrl+N → Metadata Extension) ZC_GR_ITEM_KV for that view, layer #CUSTOMER.
  3. In it: list columns MaterialDocument (10), Material (20), Plant (30), Quantity (40). Filter bar: Material, Plant. Header title: "GR item".
💡 Hint (tap)
Column: @UI.lineItem: [{ position: 10 }]. Filter: @UI.selectionField: [{ position: 10 }]. Title (above the field list): @UI.headerInfo: { typeName: 'GR item', typeNamePlural: 'GR items' }. Metadata extension shape: annotate view ZC_GR_ITEM_KV with { … FieldName; … }.
✅ Solution: the metadata extension (DDLX) 
@Metadata.layer: #CUSTOMER
@UI.headerInfo: { typeName: 'GR item', typeNamePlural: 'GR items' }
annotate view ZC_GR_ITEM_KV with
{
  @UI.lineItem: [{ position: 10 }]
  MaterialDocument;
  @UI.lineItem: [{ position: 20 }]
  @UI.selectionField: [{ position: 10 }]
  Material;
  @UI.lineItem: [{ position: 30 }]
  @UI.selectionField: [{ position: 20 }]
  Plant;
  @UI.lineItem: [{ position: 40 }]
  Quantity;
}

🔑 Why a separate file? The view keeps the data logic clean; the metadata extension holds the screen layout. Change the screen without touching the view.

🐞 If it fails

ProblemFix
Metadata extension not allowedAdd @Metadata.allowExtensions: true to the view and activate it first
Field not found in annotate viewUse the alias names of ZC_GR_ITEM_KV, with ; after each
🧠 Remember: allowExtensions in the view · layout in the DDLX · lineItem = column · selectionField = filter · headerInfo = title.
STEP 9 · S = SERVICE

🎯 Goal: see your annotations in a real Fiori app

✍️ Your task

  1. Ctrl+N → Service Definition ZUI_GR_KV: expose ZC_GR_ITEM_KV as GrItem.
  2. Ctrl+N → Service Binding ZUI_GR_KV_O4, type OData V4 – UI, service definition ZUI_GR_KV. Activate.
  3. In the binding click Publish (wait a little).
  4. Select entity GrItem → Preview. A Fiori list opens in the browser. Press Go.
✅ Solution: service definition 
@EndUserText.label: 'GR items service'
define service ZUI_GR_KV {
  expose ZC_GR_ITEM_KV as GrItem;
}

🔍 You should see

A Fiori list with 4 columns in your order, a filter bar with Material and Plant, title "GR items". 🎉 Your annotations are working.

🐞 If it fails

ProblemFix
Publish button greyed / errorActivate binding first; in shared trials publishing can take a minute, try again
List is emptyPress Go; check F8 on ZC view has rows
Columns missing / old orderMetadata extension active? Refresh the browser (old metadata cached)
🧠 Remember: define service (expose) → service binding (OData V4 UI) → Publish → Preview.
STEP 10 · D = DEBUG

🐞 How do you "debug" CDS?

CDS is SQL in the database. You can't put a breakpoint inside a CDS view. Instead you look at the data and the definition with these tools:

#Tool (in ADT)Answers the question
1F8 Data Preview + filters, follow associations"What rows does my view return?"
2SQL Console (right-click view → Open SQL Console)"Let me test a SELECT with WHERE / GROUP BY quickly"
3F2 Element Info on any field / view"Which type? Which annotations? Where does it come from?"
4Open With → Active Annotations"Which annotations are REALLY active (incl. metadata extensions)?"
5Open With → Dependency Analyzer"Which tables / views are behind this view?"
6Problems view + red ✖ markers"Why doesn't it activate?"
7ABAP debugger in the class that reads the view"What arrived in my internal table?"
8SQL trace (ST05 on-prem / ADT trace tools)"Is it slow? What SQL really ran?"

✍️ Your task: 4 debug drills

  1. 🔢 Row count drill: F8 on ZI_GR_ITEM_KV says 4. Open the SQL console and find why it's not 6 (hint: SELECT bwart, COUNT(*) … on the table).
  2. 🏷️ Annotation drill: on ZC_GR_ITEM_KV open Active Annotations. Find UI.lineItem of Material. Where does it come from (view or extension)?
  3. 💥 Break-it drill: remove the @Semantics.quantity… line in ZI_GR_ITEM_KV → activate → read the error → put it back.
  4. 🐞 Debugger drill: breakpoint (double-click left margin) on out->write in ZCL_GR_READ_KV → F9 → look at lt_vendor in the Variables view.
✅ Drill answers
1) 2 rows are 102 and 201: the where keeps only 101. 2) Active Annotations shows the source: your metadata extension (layer CUSTOMER). 3) Error about the missing reference / unit for Quantity. 4) The table shows V100 and V200 with their totals.
🎤 INTERVIEW: HOW DO YOU DEBUG A CDS VIEW?

"A CDS view runs as SQL in the database, so I can't set breakpoints in it. I check its result with the Data Preview and SQL Console in ADT, inspect fields and annotations with F2 and the Active Annotations view, see the underlying objects in the Dependency Analyzer, and fix activation errors from the Problems view. Then I debug the ABAP that consumes it to check the internal table, and use an SQL trace for performance. Once a Fiori column didn't appear; Active Annotations showed my metadata extension wasn't active."

🧠 Remember the 8 tools: "Please See Every Annotation, Do Problems Debug Slowly" = Preview, SQL console, Element info, Active annotations, Dependency analyzer, Problems view, Debugger (ABAP), SQL trace.
ANNOTATION MEMORY

🔑 The "4 K" trick: every annotation answers one question

QuestionAnnotation familiesYou used
👮 Kaun dekhe? (Who may see?)@AccessControlauthorizationCheck: #NOT_REQUIRED / #CHECK
📖 Kya hai? (What is it?)@EndUserText, @Semantics, @ObjectModel, @VDM, @Analyticslabel, quantity.unitOfMeasure
🖥️ Kaise dikhe? (How is it shown / used?)@UI, @Consumption, @Search, @MetadatalineItem, selectionField, headerInfo, allowExtensions
🗄️ Kahan rakha? (Where stored technically?)@AbapCatalogtableCategory, deliveryClass (in your tables)

⭐ The 12 annotations to know by heart

AnnotationMeansHook
@AccessControl.authorizationCheckCheck DCL roles? #CHECK / #NOT_REQUIRED👮 security guard
@EndUserText.labelDescription for humans🏷️ name tag
@Semantics.quantity.unitOfMeasureQuantity belongs to this unit field⚖️ "kitna, kis mein?"
@Semantics.amount.currencyCodeAmount belongs to this currency field💰 amount + currency = pair
@ObjectModel.text.elementWhich field is the text of this code📝 code ka naam
@VDM.viewType#BASIC / #COMPOSITE / #CONSUMPTION🏗️ which floor (I_ / C_)
@Analytics.dataCategory#CUBE / #DIMENSION / #FACT for reporting📊 analytics
@UI.lineItemColumn in a Fiori list📋 line = column in list
@UI.selectionFieldField in the filter bar🔎 select = filter
@UI.headerInfoTitle of the list / object page🎩 header = title
@Consumption.valueHelpDefinitionF4 value help for a field🔽 F4 of Fiori
@Metadata.allowExtensionsAllow annotations in a separate DDLX file📎 "baad mein jodna allowed"
🪤 Traps: 1) Quantity / amount without unit / currency annotation → activation error in view entities. 2) @UI in a DDLX does nothing without @Metadata.allowExtensions: true. 3) #CHECK without a DCL role → warning, and rows may be filtered later. 4) @AbapCatalog.sqlViewName is only for the old define view, not for view entities.
🧠 Remember the 4 K: Kaun dekhe (AccessControl) · Kya hai (EndUserText, Semantics, ObjectModel, VDM, Analytics) · Kaise dikhe (UI, Consumption, Search, Metadata) · Kahan rakha (AbapCatalog). 🔑🔑🔑🔑

😂 Final test: write from memory (no peeking!)

  1. ✍️ On paper or in ADT, write ZI_GR_ITEM_KV again from memory: 2 annotations, keys, aliases, quantity with unit, where, association _PO.
  2. ✍️ Write the metadata extension with 2 columns and 1 filter field.
  3. 🗣️ Say the "4 K" families out loud, with 2 annotations each.
  4. 🗣️ Say the 8 debug tools ("Please See Every Annotation, Do Problems Debug Slowly").
🎤 INTERVIEW: TELL ME ABOUT A CDS VIEW YOU BUILT

"On my BTP trial I built a small GR model: a basic view ZI_GR_ITEM_KV on a GR item table filtered to movement type 101, with CamelCase aliases, a quantity annotated with its unit, and an association to a PO header view. I added a parameter view by plant, read it from a class with a path expression and SUM by vendor, then built a consumption view with UI annotations in a metadata extension and published it as an OData V4 service to preview a Fiori list. I debugged it with Data Preview, the SQL Console and Active Annotations."

⚡ Cheat sheet. Everything from the lab on one screen.

☕ Steps: Tea First, Very Cold, Add Pepper, Use Sugar, Drink

Table → Fill (F9) → View (F8) → Calculate (CASE) → Association (_PO) → Parameter ($parameters) → UI (DDLX) → Service (binding, publish, preview) → Debug.

🧱 View skeleton

@AccessControl.authorizationCheck: #NOT_REQUIRED
@EndUserText.label: '…'
define view entity ZI_X with parameters p : type
  as select from source
  association [0..1] to ZI_Y as _Y on $projection.A = _Y.A
{ key f as Alias, case … end as Txt, _Y }
where … $parameters.p

🔑 4 K annotation families

👮 Kaun dekhe: AccessControl · 📖 Kya hai: EndUserText, Semantics (quantity / amount), ObjectModel, VDM, Analytics · 🖥️ Kaise dikhe: UI (lineItem, selectionField, headerInfo), Consumption (valueHelp), Search, Metadata (allowExtensions) · 🗄️ Kahan rakha: AbapCatalog.

🐞 Debug: Please See Every Annotation, Do Problems Debug Slowly

F8 Preview · SQL Console · F2 Element info · Active Annotations · Dependency Analyzer · Problems view · ABAP Debugger on the consumer · SQL trace. No breakpoints inside CDS.

🪤 Traps: keys first · no comma after last field · where on table = table names, on view = aliases · quantity needs unit · expose the association · DDLX needs allowExtensions · shared trial = unique names.
📨
IDOC · BUILT FROM SAP4BEGINNERS VIDEO + OUR LESSONS

IDoc: setup, create, find, fix, test

How SAP sends and receives business documents, and what you do when one fails.

😫 PROBLEMSAP must swap POs, orders, invoices and materials with other systems that store data differently.
💡 IDEAPack the data into one fixed "envelope" both sides understand, and keep a log of every step.
🔧 HOWIDoc = control record + data records + status records. Track in WE02/WE09, fix in BD87, test in WE19.
➡️ NEXTLevels 7 and 8 cover setup and creation. Then OData, the modern alternative (Module 11).

LEARNLevel by level

Part 1 · IDoc basics (Q64) Part 2 · Find, fix, test Part 3 · Setup and create Part 4 · Tricks and story

🌉 Interface and IDoc

An interface is the bridge between two systems. Data crossing it travels as an IDoc (Intermediate Document): a fixed structure both sides agree on.

🧾
You create a PO in ME21N. The vendor's system must get it. SAP packs EKKO/EKPO data into an ORDERS IDoc and sends it. The vendor's system reads it and creates a sales order.
TermMeaning
📤 OutboundIDoc leaves SAP (PO to vendor)
📥 InboundIDoc comes into SAP (customer order, material master)
🔗 ALEApplication Link Enabling: SAP ↔ SAP
🌐 EDIElectronic Data Interchange: SAP ↔ non-SAP
🧱 MiddlewareTranslator in between, e.g. SAP PI/PO or CPI
🧠 Remember: interface = bridge, IDoc = fixed envelope. ALE = SAP ↔ SAP, EDI = SAP ↔ non-SAP. Your project used files in AL11 instead.

✉️ Every IDoc = 3 parts (tap)

💡 EDI + DC / D4 / DS = Control, Data, Status.
WordMeaningExample
Message typeWhat business documentORDERS, INVOIC, MATMAS
Basic typeIts structure versionORDERS05, INVOIC02, MATMAS05
SegmentOne block of fieldsE1EDK01 header, E1EDP01 item
Partner typeWho the partner isLS system, LI vendor, KU customer
💡 Segment names: K = Kopf (header), P = Position (item). Same letters as EKKO / EKPO.
🧠 Remember: EDIDC control (envelope), EDID4 data (letter), EDIDS status (tracking). Message type = what, basic type = structure, segment K = header, P = item.

🚦 Status codes

01–49 = outbound, 50–75 = inbound. In BD87: 🟢 done, 🟡 waiting, 🔴 error.

CodeMeaning
🟡 30Ready for dispatch (waiting to send)
🟢 03Passed to port: sent
🔴 02Error passing to port
🔴 26Syntax error (structure wrong)
🔴 29Error in ALE service (e.g. no partner profile)
✏️ 32IDoc was edited
CodeMeaning
🟡 50IDoc added (arrived)
🟡 64Ready to pass to application
🟡 62Passed to application
🟢 53Posted: order / material created
🔴 51Not posted: business error
⛔ 68Error, no further processing (closed)
✏️ 69IDoc was edited
📁 70Original of an edited IDoc (kept as backup)
50→64→62→53 ✅or51 ❌
🧠 Remember: 01–49 outbound, 50+ inbound. 30 waiting, 03 sent, 53 posted, 51 error.

🔎 Find an IDoc

T-codeUse it when
WE02 / WE05You know the IDoc no., date, message type or partner
WE09You only know a business value inside it, like a PO or material number
BD87See all IDocs grouped by status with counts, then process them
  1. WE09: enter a short date range (long ranges are slow in real systems).
  2. Fill control fields you know: direction, basic type, message type, partner.
  3. In data record search, enter the value, e.g. PO 4500001234.
  4. Execute → open the IDoc → read control, data and status records.
🧠 Remember: WE02 / WE05 list, WE09 search a value inside, BD87 by status + reprocess.

🛠️ Fix a status 51

51 is almost always a business data problem. Read the status text, then check master data.

Error textCheck
Material for item 10 could not be identifiedMM03: does the material exist?
Sold-to party not maintained for sales areaCustomer in BP / XD03 for that sales org
Customer blocked for ordersAsk business to remove the block
✅ Trick from the video: open a successful 53 IDoc of the same type and compare field by field with the failed one.
  1. Fix the master data (best) or edit the segment: data record → double-click segment → Change → Save.
  2. Editing sets status 69; the original is kept as 70.
  3. BD87: select the IDoc → Process. Good → 53. Still wrong → 51 again.
⚠️ Real projects: editing a live IDoc changes audited business data. Get approval first, and prefer fixing master data and reprocessing.
🧠 Remember: 51 = business data problem: read the text, check master data, compare with a 53, fix, reprocess in BD87.

🧪 WE19: test without touching the original

  1. WE19: enter an existing IDoc no. → Execute. You get a copy.
  2. Change or delete segments in the copy.
  3. Click Standard inbound → a new IDoc is created and processed.
  4. The original stays as your reference.

👨‍💻 What an ABAP developer adds

  • 🐞 WE19 → Inbound function module → run in debug mode to step through e.g. IDOC_INPUT_ORDERS.
  • ➕ Extra fields: extend the basic type (WE30, segments in WE31) and fill them in a user exit or BADI.
  • 🗺️ Mapping data to segments is developer work; functional consultants mostly monitor and fix.
🎤 INTERVIEW

"In support I monitored inbound ORDERS and MATMAS IDocs in BD87. For status 51 I read the status text, for example material not found for item 10, checked the master data in MM03, compared the failed IDoc with a successful one of the same type, and reprocessed it in BD87 once fixed. For testing I copied the IDoc in WE19 and debugged the inbound function module."

🧠 Remember: WE19 = copy + change + test + debug the inbound FM. The original stays untouched.

⚙️ Setup: done once, before any IDoc moves

🧾
Before PO 4500001234 can go to vendor 100200 as an IDoc, SAP must know who the vendor is, how to reach them, and which format to use. Like saving a contact before you send a WhatsApp.
StepT-codeSimple meaning
1️⃣ Logical systemBD54 + SCC4Give each system a name, e.g. S4DCLNT100, and attach it to your client
2️⃣ RFC destinationSM59The road to the other SAP system (ALE only)
3️⃣ PortWE21The door: tRFC (SAP to SAP), File, or XML/HTTP
4️⃣ Partner profileWE20The contact card: partner + message type + port + basic type
5️⃣ Distribution modelBD64ALE only: who sends which message type to whom

🧩 Partner profile in detail

  • 👤 Partner type: LS other SAP system, LI vendor, KU customer.
  • 📤 Outbound line: message type ORDERS → port → basic type ORDERS05. For PO output, also output type NEU.
  • 📥 Inbound line: message type → process code.
💡 Process code = which program handles it. Inbound ORDE → function module IDOC_INPUT_ORDERS (creates the sales order). See WE42 (inbound) and WE41 (outbound).
⚠️ Most common setup error: no partner profile line for that message type → outbound status 29 (or 56 inbound).
🧠 Remember: Logical system BD54 → RFC SM59 → port WE21 → partner profile WE20 → model BD64. Process code links message to FM.

🐣 Create: 3 ways an IDoc is born

🛣️ The full outbound road (PO example)

ME21N save→Output NEU→IDoc ORDERS→30 ready→Port→03 sent ✅

🛬 The full inbound road (sales order)

File / RFC in→50 added→Partner profile→Process code ORDE→IDOC_INPUT_ORDERS→53 order ✅
✅ ABAP developer view: outbound programs fill segments and call MASTER_IDOC_DISTRIBUTE. Inbound work happens in the IDOC_INPUT_* function module, which you debug from WE19.
🎤 INTERVIEW

"For PO to vendor, the PO output type NEU with EDI medium created an ORDERS05 IDoc. I checked the setup: port in WE21, vendor partner profile in WE20 with message type ORDERS and basic type ORDERS05. When IDocs stuck in status 29 it was a missing partner profile line; after adding it I resent them from BD87."

🧠 Remember: Born 3 ways: output (NACE, NEU), change pointers (BD50 on, BD21 send), manual (BD10 / BD12 / BD14).

😂 T-code tricks you won't forget

🙋 WE = "We Explore IDocs": see, set up, build, test.
📢 BD = "Bhai, Distribute!": send master data, fix bad ones.

🏘️ WE number families

FamilyNicknameCodes
WE0x👀 The nosy neighbour: only looksWE02 WE05 list · WE09 search inside (9 = 🔍)
WE1x🧪 The jugaad lab: try without breakingWE19 copy, edit, test, debug
WE2x💍 Rishta first, then venueWE20 partner (who) → WE21 port (how)
WE3x🏗️ The builderWE30 IDoc type · WE31 segment
WE4x📮 Send first, receive laterWE41 outbound · WE42 inbound process code
WE6x📖 The libraryWE60 IDoc documentation
WE8x🏷️ Naamkaran, then linkWE81 message type · WE82 link to basic type

📢 BD gang

  • 🐸 Frog jump by 2: BD10 material → BD12 customer → BD14 vendor (M-C-V). Odd numbers 11/13/15 = get instead of send.
  • 📹 CCTV: BD50 switches on change pointers (recording), BD21 sends the footage (changed data).
  • 🩺 BD87 = "Bad Docs" doctor: heals and reprocesses failed IDocs.
  • 🗺️ BD64 = the family map: who sends what to whom.

👯 WE02 vs WE05: the twins

Same body, two names. Both run report RSEIDOC2. Long ago one was "display" and one was "lists". SAP merged them but kept both names, like dadaji's old Nokia kept safe in the drawer: nobody uses it differently, but nobody throws it away either. 📱

🕵️ T-code detective: SE93 shows the program behind any T-code. Two codes look the same? Check them in SE93.
🎤 INTERVIEW

"WE02 and WE05 both show the IDoc list and run the same report, RSEIDOC2. SAP kept both for backward compatibility. I use WE02 for daily checks, WE09 to search by a value like a PO number, WE19 to test and debug, and BD87 to reprocess. I check any unclear T-code in SE93."

📦 Status story: IDoc Express courier

🚧 50 is the border: below 50 = parcel leaving SAP. 50 and above = parcel arriving in SAP.

📤 Sending a parcel (outbound)

CodeCourier story
🟡 01Parcel packed
🟡 30Waiting at the gate for the truck
🟢 03Truck left the gate. Gone! 🚚
🔴 02Truck stuck at the gate (port problem)
🔴 29No address on the parcel (partner profile missing)
🔴 26Box is broken (wrong structure / syntax)

📥 Receiving a parcel (inbound)

CodeCourier story
🟡 50Parcel reached the godown
🔴 56"Who sent this?" Unknown sender (no partner profile)
🟡 64Sorted, ready for the clerk
🟡 62Handed to the clerk
🟢 53Clerk signed it. Order created. "53 = Sab Theek" 🎉
🔴 51Clerk refused: "material not found!" "51 = 1 problem"

✏️ Special parcels

  • ✍️ You scribbled on the label (edited): 69 in, 32 out.
  • 📄 Photocopy of the original label kept safe: 70 in, 33 out (edited number + 1).
  • 🗄️ "Never open" room (no more processing): 68 in, 31 out.
✅ Neighbour rule: fail sits next to pass at the same step. 03✅ / 02❌ at the gate. 53✅ / 51❌ at the clerk.
👨‍💻 Who writes the status? The inbound function module (e.g. IDOC_INPUT_ORDERS) fills table IDOC_STATUS with 53 + order number, or 51 + error text. SAP saves it in EDIDS. All status texts live in WE47.

🎮 Your turn (answer first, then tap)

🎤 FULL MODULE ANSWER

"An IDoc is SAP's standard envelope for exchanging business documents, through ALE between SAP systems or EDI with non-SAP partners. It has a control record in EDIDC, data segments in EDID4 and status records in EDIDS; outbound statuses are 01 to 49 and inbound 50 and above, with 53 posted and 51 an error. I find IDocs in WE02 or WE09, fix business errors in master data and reprocess in BD87, and test or debug with WE19. Setup needs a logical system, RFC destination, port in WE21 and partner profile in WE20. My own project used file interfaces through AL11, and IDocs are the standard alternative I'd choose for SAP-to-SAP flows."

VIDEONotes on the source

📺 SAP4BEGINNERS: "Master SAP IDoc Process in Real-Time" (36 min, Hindi). Taught from a functional consultant view.

  • ✅ Very good: WE09 search, BD87 colours, reading the 3 records, fixing 51, WE19 copy test.
  • 🔧 Status numbers "17" and "73" in the video are mis-spoken. Edited IDoc = 69 (inbound) / 32 (outbound); original = 70 / 33. 73 = archived.
  • ➕ Not in the video, so added as Levels 7 and 8: setup (WE21, WE20, BD64, process code) and creation (output, change pointers, BD10).

DOHands-on

🏢 Office system only. BTP trial has no IDoc, no SAP GUI.

  1. BD87: last 7 days → see counts per status.
  2. Open one 53 and one 51 IDoc of the same message type.
  3. Compare their data records segment by segment.
  4. WE19: copy the 51 one, fix a field, Standard inbound → new IDoc number.

TESTQuick check

1. Inbound IDoc posted successfully?

03 = outbound sent. 53 = inbound posted. 51 = not posted.

2. You only know the PO number inside the IDoc. Which T-code?

WE09 searches values inside data records.

3. You edit a 51 IDoc. Its new status?

69 = edited. Original saved as 70. 68 = closed, no more processing.

4. T-code for the partner profile?

WE20 = partner profile (who, which message). WE21 = port. BD64 = distribution model.

5. Which table holds the status history of an IDoc?

EDIDC control, EDID4 data, EDIDS status.

6. Standard SAP-to-SAP way instead of your AL11 files?

ALE = SAP ↔ SAP. EDI = with non-SAP partners.
Answer all 6 first.
⚡ Quick revise. Read top to bottom in 2 minutes. Forgot something? Switch to 📖 Learn.

📨 IDoc basics

  • IDoc = fixed "envelope" for business data. 📤 outbound (PO to vendor) · 📥 inbound (order in).
  • 🔗 ALE = SAP ↔ SAP · 🌐 EDI = SAP ↔ non-SAP.
  • 3 records: EDIDC control · EDID4 data · EDIDS status.
  • Message type ORDERS · basic type ORDERS05 · segments E1EDK01 (K header), E1EDP01 (P item).

🚦 Status (50 = border)

📤 30 ready · 03 sent ✅ · 02 port error · 26 syntax · 29 no partner profile · 32 edited

📥 50 arrived · 64 ready · 62 passed · 53 posted ✅ · 51 error · 56 unknown sender · 68 closed · 69 edited (original 70)

🔧 Daily T-codes

JobT-code
List IDocsWE02 / WE05 (twins, report RSEIDOC2)
Search by value insideWE09
ReprocessBD87
Test copy + debugWE19
Port · partner profileWE21 · WE20 (LS / LI / KU)
Logical system · RFC · modelBD54 · SM59 · BD64
Process codesWE41 out · WE42 in (ORDE → IDOC_INPUT_ORDERS)

🐣 Created by: output NEU (NACE) · change pointers (BD50 on, BD21 send) · manual BD10/BD12/BD14 (M-C-V).

🛠️ Fix 51: read status text → check master data (MM03) → compare with a 53 → fix → BD87.

😂 Tricks: WE = "We Explore" · BD = "Bhai Distribute" · 53 = "Sab Theek" · 51 = "1 problem" · BD10/12/14 = frog jump by 2.
🎤 30-SECOND ANSWER

"In support I monitored inbound ORDERS and MATMAS IDocs in BD87. For status 51 I read the status text, for example material not found for item 10, checked the master data in MM03, compared the failed IDoc with a successful one of the same type, and reprocessed it in BD87 once fixed. For testing I copied the IDoc in WE19 and debugged the inbound function module."

💼 Order to cash

🔢 Last digits = action

EndsActionExamples
01➕ CreateME21N, VA01, VF01, MM01
02✏️ ChangeME22N, VA02, VF02, MM02
03👁️ Display onlyME23N, VA03, VF03, MM03, FB03
N🆕 New one-page screenME21N replaced ME21

🔠 First letters = area

PrefixAreaHint
MEPurchasingEinkauf
MMMaterial masterMaterial
MIMIGO goods movement, MIRO invoiceMM inventory / invoice

VLDeliveryLieferung

FBAccounting docFinanzbuchhaltung
SEDeveloper toolsSE11 DDIC, SE38 editor, SE37 FM, SE24 class
💡 ME23N = purchasing + display + new screen = "display PO".

📦 Movement type = kind of stock move

Every MIGO posting has a 3-digit code in MSEG-BWART. It decides which stock changes and which accounts post.

CodeMeaning
101📥 GR for a PO (normal GR)
102↩️ Reverse a 101 (posted by mistake)
122🔙 Return to vendor (bad goods)
201📤 Issue to cost center
261🏭 Issue to production order
311🔁 Storage location transfer
561🆕 Initial stock upload
601🚚 Goods issue for sales delivery
💡 1xx receipt · 2xx issue · 3xx transfer · 5xx no-PO receipt · 6xx sales. Odd + 1 = undo: 101→102.
" GR report: only PO receipts
SELECT mblnr, mjahr, ebeln, menge
  FROM mseg
  WHERE bwart = '101'
  INTO TABLE @DATA(lt_gr).

🏷️ Field names decoded

FieldGermanMeaning

EBELP…PositionPO item

BUKRSBuchungskreisCompany code

LIFNRLieferanten-Nr.Vendor no.
KUNNRKunden-Nr.Customer no.
MATNRMaterial-Nr.Material no.
MENGEMengeQuantity
BUDATBuchungsdatumPosting date
WRBTRBetragAmount (doc currency)
BWARTBewegungsartMovement type
💡 NR = number · JAHR = year · DAT = date.

TESTQuick check

1. GR header with posting date?

K = Kopf. Join MKPF + MSEG on MBLNR + MJAHR.

2. What does VF03 do?

VF = billing, 03 = display.

3. Reverse a wrong 101 GR?

Odd + 1 = undo. 122 = return to vendor.
Answer all 3 first.
